本次報告：法說會

儒鴻 (1476 TT) 
不利因素消退，營運展望正向

永豐觀點 
隨著美國期中選舉結果出爐，關稅及戰爭兩大不利因素有望逐步消退，有利成衣產
業評價回升。公司訂單能見度維持六個月，新興品牌客戶訂單動能強勁，推升營收
及獲利表現，後市展望持續正向，研究部上調儒鴻目標價，維持買進投資評等。 
 投資評價與建議 
投資建議維持買進，目標價上調至 500 元：儒鴻 3Q26 毛利率表現優於預期，反
映新興品牌客戶訂單動能強勁，改善產品組合，且原材料成本壓力舒緩，帶動獲利
表現回升。展望後市，儒鴻訂單能見度維持六個月，新興品牌客戶訂單動能持續成
長強勁，既有品牌客戶亦維持穩定回補庫存力道，營運展望正向。研究部預估公司
2026/2027 稅後 EPS 25.39/27.79 元，上調本益比評價至 18X，依 2027 之 EPS
進行評價，目標價上調至 500 元，投資評等維持買進，依公司去年股利配發率
74.63%估算，以昨日收盤價來看，目前約具備 4.9%殖利率，應具備下檔殖利率保
護。

ESG 評析 
儒鴻企業永續評鑑整體為 B+等，於 SASB 產業永續議題與跨產業五大永續面向平
均得分分別為 B 等與 B+等。 
SinoPac+ ESG 評鑑系統評等

資料來源：SinoPac+ ESG 評等 (依循 2024 SinoPac+企業永續評鑑方法學) 
註 1：資誠永續發展服務股份有限公司僅於ＥＳＧ評鑑系統方法學建置過程中，就評分指標提供
專業意見，對於評分結果及評估報告內容之完整性及真實性，不負擔保責任，亦不對閱讀或使用
本評估報告之第三方負任何責任。 
註 2：永豐投顧已獲 SASB 授權，於研究中使用 SASB 重大性地圖之一般議題分類及揭露主題。
SinoPac Securities Investment Service licenses and applies the SASB Materiality Map® 
General Issue Categories and Disclosure Topics in our work.

### Page 2

2

儒鴻為國內成衣業龍頭廠商：儒鴻成立於 1977 年，主要生產彈性針織布與運動服
飾，並垂直整合染整、織布到成衣製造，具有一條龍整合優勢，此外公司持續提升
布料研發和服飾設計能力，積極研發出具流行性、運動性、休閒性、環保性等功能
性之布料，廣泛應用於高級時裝、運動服、休閒服裝、內睡衣、韻律裝等多樣化產
品系列，並銷售至全球知名品牌及零售商，公司 2025 年營收結構為：成衣 66.4%、
針織布 33.6%，銷售地區以美洲為主，主要客戶包括 Nike、Lululemon、Under 
Armour 等知名大廠。 
3Q26 EPS 6.36 元，符合預期：儒鴻 3Q26 營收 105.72 億元（+3.9% QoQ，
+8.8% YoY），略優於預期，主因新興品牌客戶訂單需求強勁，推升營收表現，毛
利率 31.18%（+1.17pts. QoQ，+2.52pts. YoY），優於預期，除因新興品牌客戶
訂單增長，帶動產品組合改善外，儒鴻持續與客戶進行議價，以轉嫁原材料成本上
漲，推升毛利率表現回升，稅後淨利 17.4 億元（+14.2% QoQ，+7.3% YoY），符
合預期，其中，營業費用高於預期，主因儒鴻獲利強勁，認列季獎金費用較預期高
所致，3Q26 稅後 EPS 6.36 元，符合預期。

### Page 3

3

10 月營收符合預期，4Q26 營收預估季增：儒鴻 10 月營收 34 億元，-3.1% MoM，
+10.7% YoY，符合預期。展望 4Q26，新興品牌客戶訂單動能強勁，將抵銷布料
淡季造成的營收下滑力道，預期 4Q26 營收將季增，且因產品組合持續優化，及原
材料成本陸續轉嫁，毛利率表現有望穩定回升，推升獲利表現，基此，研究部預估
4Q26 營收 106.2 億元（+0.5％ QoQ，+14.6％ YoY），毛利率 31.5%（+0.32 
pts. QoQ，+1.31 pts. YoY），稅後淨利 18.3 億元（+5.2% QoQ，+15.9％ YoY），
稅後 EPS 6.68 元。 
不利因素消退，產業評價回升，維持買進投資建議：展望後市，儒鴻訂單能見度維
持約六個月，其中，新興品牌客戶訂單動能持續成長強勁，既有品牌客戶亦維持穩
定回補庫存力道。隨著美國期中選舉結果出爐，關稅政策、美伊戰爭此些不利公司
營運及評價的兩大變數皆有望消退，儘管公司維持先前營收及毛利率指引，但隨著
大環境不確定性因素下降、產品組合持續轉佳、原物料成本下跌及向下轉嫁、垂直
整合比重提升，應有利營收及毛利率表現穩步走升，推升獲利表現。公司 2027 年
預計將藉由產能去瓶頸，提升約個位數水準，印尼新廠預期將於 4Q27-1Q28 投
表三：季度成衣營收趨勢

### Page 4

4

產，規劃月產能為 180 萬件，將分兩期完成。綜合來看，研究部評估儒鴻訂單動
能維持穩健，隨著公司持續導入新產品及新客人加入，有利儒鴻營運表現提升，而
隨著原材料成本成功轉嫁及產品組合持續好轉，毛利率預期將穩步回升，研究部維
持正向看待儒鴻後市獲利表現。研究部預估 2026 年營收 409 億元（+7.7％YoY），
毛利率 31.21%，稅後淨利 69.7 億元（+26.3％YoY），稅後 EPS 25.39 元，預估
2027 年營收 442.3 億元（+8.2％YoY），毛利率 31.5%，稅後淨利 76.3 億元（+9.5
％YoY），稅後 EPS 27.79 元。隨著大環境不利因素消退，成衣產業評價應回升，
儒鴻過往本益比區間多位於 12-30 倍，研究部上調儒鴻本益比評價至 18X，依 2027
之 EPS 進行評價，目標價上調至 500 元，投資評等維持買進，依公司去年股利配
發率 74.63%估算，以昨日收盤價來看，目前約具備 4.9%殖利率，應具備下檔殖
利率保護。

2  兆豐國際商業銀行 http://www.megabank.com.tw/
上週重要市場資訊
⚫ 美國聯準會如市場預期，維持政策利率不變。決策官員仍對通膨保持擔憂，也承
認美國經濟正以「強勁速度」成長。聯準會主席 Kevin Warsh 在會後談話中，並
未明確排除進一步緊縮政策的可能，激勵市場對 12 月升息的預期升溫。數據方
面，美國第三季 GDP 年化成長率達 3.4%，高於前值的 2.2%，亦優於市場預期
的 3.2%，印證美國經濟動能強勁。此外，9 月 PCE 與核心 PCE 符合預期，但凸
顯通膨壓力持續偏高。在「通膨高企」與「經濟強韌」雙重因素驅動下，市場預
期聯準會將於 12 月升息，為美元指數提供有力支撐。惟近期受 AI 產業股票波
動加劇影響，部分資金轉入美債避險，導致美元指數在高檔面臨調節性賣壓。
⚫ 儘管受能源價格走揚影響，9 月歐元區通膨率升至 3.8%，但在薪資壓力維持溫
和，且歐洲央行尚未觀察到第二輪通膨效應全面擴散的情況下，歐洲央行上週決
議維持政策利率不變。通膨回升令市場預期歐洲央行將在下次會議升息，為歐元

提供部分支撐，但考量歐元區經濟成長表現仍落後美國，在美元基本面強勁的抑
制下，歐元反彈力道相對有限。目前歐元最大的潛在風險在於部分國家的債券市
場壓力尚未消散，市場對歐洲財政風險仍保持高度警戒。
⚫ 日本央行維持政策利率於 1.25%不變，並且首次明確表示已達成其 2%的通膨目
標；過去日本央行雖認為通膨改善，但始終強調尚未穩定達標，如今首次正式承
認達成 2%目標，代表未來升息門檻進一步下降。此外，日本央行將經濟成長預
測維持在 0.7%至 0.8%，並小幅上修 CPI 預測，令市場預期日央 12 月有望再次
升息，日本貨幣政策正常化將有助日圓結束疲軟走勢。

3  兆豐國際商業銀行 http://www.megabank.com.tw/ 
 美元兌新台幣匯率走勢  
Nov/2/2026 
＜上週走勢＞ 
    上週台幣匯價高點為 32.390，低點在 32.676，以 32.478 作收，較前一周收盤價
升值 3 分。 
上週台幣先升後貶，周初，市場等待聯準會利率決議，交投偏謹慎觀望，而外
資在前周大幅匯出之後，轉為雙向進出，台幣止跌回穩，最高來到 32.390 的周內高
點；然而周間，美國科技股財報表現失色，台股賣壓沉重，外資賣超台股並再度轉
為大幅匯出，台幣走貶，最低來到 32.676，不過匯價逢高吸引出口商賣壓出籠，加
上央行提供流動性，台幣貶幅收斂，終場在央行調節之下收在 32.478。

＜未來分析＞ 
    預估本週台幣偏弱震盪。就國際市場來看，聯準會 10 月利率決策維持利率不
變，但有兩名官員主張升息 1 碼，且聯準會表示通膨仍處於高檔，顯示聯準會對通
膨仍保持警戒，市場預期美國利率將維持高檔，台美利差仍維持在相對高位，不利
台幣表現；另一方面，美國科技股財報表現不如預期，引發市場對 AI 投資回報的疑
慮，對台股也帶來壓力。就台灣市場來看，外資持續調節台股並大幅匯出，10 月累
計賣超將近兆元，資金匯出壓力大，不過一方面央行持續進場調節，加上匯價逢高
預計吸引出口商進場拋匯，料將限制台幣貶值速度。綜合以上，預估本週台幣走勢
偏弱。 
＜預估本週＞ 美元兌台幣：32.300-32.800

美元兌人民幣匯率走勢  
Nov/2/2026 
＜上週走勢＞ 
上週人民幣持續走升。周初市場等待美國聯準會利率決議，交投偏向觀望，境
外人民幣主要於 6.6600 上方震盪；周間聯準會如預期維持利率不變，美元指數自 102
上方回落，加上中國出口結匯需求持續提供支撐，境外人民幣突破 6.6600 整數關口；
週五人行中間價續創近三年九個月新高，最高來到 6.7138，顯示中國人行允許人民
幣溫和升值的走勢，境外人民幣一度升破 6.6500 整數關口，最高來到 6.6487，終場
收在 6.6528，週線升值 162 點，境內人民幣則收在 6.6558，週線升值 146 點。

＜未來分析＞ 
     預估本週人民幣偏升。就中國國內市場來看，10 月官方製造業 PMI 降至 49.3，
重新落入景氣收縮區間，反映中國內需及製造業動能仍有待改善，不過 9 月出口年
增 36.3%，出口商結匯需求持續提供人民幣支撐，加上人行中間價續創近三年九個
月新高，預計將持續帶動人民幣走升；就國際市場來看，聯準會 10 月利率決策維持
利率不變，但有兩名官員主張升息 1 碼，顯示聯準會對通膨仍保持警戒，市場預期
美國利率將維持高檔，美中利差維持在相對高位，將限制人民幣升值的速度與幅度；
最後就技術面來看，USD/CNH 日線圖中，各天期均線向下發散，匯價維持在五日均
線之下交投，均線呈現空頭排列，顯示空方勢力較盛，綜合以上，預估本週人民幣
偏升。

＜預估本週＞ 美元兌人民幣：6.6200-6.6700

美元兌日圓匯率走勢  
         Nov/02/2026

＜上週走勢＞ 
上週 USD/JPY 上漲。週初，美伊衝突仍陷僵局支撐美元避險買盤，USD/JPY 最
高來到 158.34；週間，聯準會公布利率維持利率不變，USD/JPY 持續在 158 附近盤
整；臨近週末，日本央行公布利率維持不變並釋放偏鷹訊號，USD/JPY 最低來到
156.94，終場收在 157.53，週線上漲 72 點。 
＜未來分析＞ 
  預估本週 USD/JPY 區間震盪，主要原因如下：一、日本央行於上周五宣布利
率維持在 1.25%不變，並表示隨著基礎通膨率大致達到 2%，央行必須持續控制通
膨穩定於此目標，且再次強調貨幣政策已進入更為緊縮階段，未來每隔數月即可能
升息。此外，日本央行在季度報告中，亦將截至 2028 年 3 月財年的核心消費者通
膨預估自三個月前預測的 2.4%上調至 2.6%。日本央行偏鷹立場令市場預期日本最
快將於 12 月再次升息，預計將有利日圓表現。二、聯準會宣布維持利率於 3.75%-
4%區間不變，惟因通膨仍處高位，聯準會重申將致力實現 2%的長期通膨區間， 
FedWatch 顯示 12 月升息機率約七成，美元持穩，預計將限縮 USD/JPY 下行空
間。三、就技術面而言，在日線圖中，USD/JPY 各天期均線趨平，近期匯價大致
在 156 至 158 間震盪，匯價暫無明確方向性。綜合以上，預估 USD/JPY 本週呈現
區間震盪的可能性較高。

美元兌歐元匯率走勢  
             Nov/02/2026

＜上週走勢＞ 
  上週 EUR/USD 下跌。週初，美伊衝突持續以及法國政經情勢動盪等情況下，
歐元走跌，最低來到 1.1111；週間，聯準會和歐洲央行相繼公布利率決議均維持不
變，歐洲央行發表言論偏鷹派，歐元止跌回升至 1.1214；臨近週末，歐元區公布經
濟數據好壞參半，歐元自高點回落，終場收在 1.1158，週線下跌 41 點。 
＜未來分析＞ 
  預估本週 EUR/USD 區間震盪，主要原因如下：一、歐洲央行如市場預期宣布
存款利率維持在 2.5%不變，並警告能源成本持續上漲將對通膨造成上行風險，暗示
可能在 12 月再次升息，升息預期升溫預計將支撐歐元走勢。二、歐元區第三季 GDP
年增 1.3%，高於市場預期的 1.1%，顯示在能源價格持續上漲的不利因素下，經濟仍
展現韌性，預計有利歐元表現。三、聯準會宣布維持利率在 3.75%-4%不變，並重申
致力控制通膨的立場。此外，美國公布第三季 GDP 成長 3.4%，優於市場預期的 3.2%，
9 月核心 PCE 年增率自前月的 3%上升至 3.1%，在美國經濟強勁及通膨仍高的情況
下，市場預期聯準會將於 12 月升息一碼，美元持穩，預計將限縮歐元走升空間。四、
技術面而言，在日線圖中，EUR/USD 各天期均線向下發散，不過近期匯價在多次向
下測試 1.1120 附近均未有效跌破，顯示下方仍有買盤支撐。綜合以上，預估 EUR/USD
本週呈現區間震盪的可能性較高。

膨容忍度變寬之態度，惟市場對央行升息預期並未完全消除，買盤追價動力有

券區間為 1.80%~1.85%，10 年券區間為 1.99%~2.04%。

実質ＧＤＰ成長率の推移(年度）(前年比）
(資料）内閣府経済社会総合研究所「四半期別ＧＤＰ速報」
(年度）
予測

＜実質成長率：2026年度 0.9％、2027年度 1.3％、2028年度 1.1％を予想＞

１. 2026年 7-9月期の実質 GDPは、民間消費の落ち込みを主因として前期比年率▲0.1％と 4
四半期ぶりのマイナス成長となった。 
２. 物価上昇ペースの加速に伴う実質購買力の低下を主因として民間消費が低迷することな
どから、2026年 10-12月期、2027年 1-3月期の実質 GDP成長率は 0％台前半から半ばに
とどまることが予想される。2026年度後半の日本経済は低空飛行が続く可能性が高い。 
３. 2027年度に入ると、飲食料品の消費税率引き下げに伴う物価上昇率の低下から家計の実
質購買力が改善し、民間消費が回復に向かうほか、原材料価格の低下に伴う企業収益の
改善を受けて設備投資の増勢ペースが加速することが見込まれる。 
４. 実質 GDP成長率は 2026年度が 0.9％、2027年度が 1.3％、2028年度が 1.1％と予想する。
消費税率引き下げによる実質 GDPへの影響は 2026年度が▲0.1％、2027年度が 0.3％と
試算する。 
５. 消費者物価上昇率（生鮮食品を除く総合）は輸入物価の上昇が食料、日用品など幅広い
品目に波及し、2026年度末にかけては 3％台まで加速するが、飲食料品の消費税率引き
下げを受けて、2027年度には 1％台まで鈍化するだろう。消費者物価上昇率（生鮮食品
を除く総合）は、2026年度が 2.4％、2027年度が 1.5％、2028年度が 2.0％と予想する。 
ニッセイ基礎研究所 2026-11-17

2026～2028年度経済見通し（26年 11月）

経済研究部 経済調査部長 斎藤  太郎

### Page 2

（中東情勢の影響） 
中東情勢の緊迫化を受けて一時大幅に減少した日本の原油輸入量は、代替調達の進展によって持
ち直し、7月以降は前年の水準を上回って推移している。 
財務省の「貿易統計」で原油輸入の動向を確認すると、2026年 3月は攻撃前にホルムズ海峡を通
過・出港していた原油が日本に到着したため、影響はまだ限定的であったが、4、5月には中東産を
中心に原油輸入量が前年比で 6割前後の大幅減少となった。その後、ホルムズ海峡を経由しない中
東産に加え、米国を中心とした代替調達が進んだことから、6月には減少幅が大きく縮小し、7月
以降は、原油輸入量が前年の水準を上回っている。2025年時点で中東産が約 7割を占めていたナフ
サについては、中東からの輸入量は減少が続いているものの、米国、ペルー、アルジェリアからの
輸入量が急増するなど代替調達が進展している。

＜原油＞ ＜ナフサ＞

### Page 3

原油輸入に占める中東の割合（数量ベース）は 2025年時点で 94.0％だったが、足もとでは 60％
台まで低下し、米国の割合が 2025年の 3.8％から 30％台まで上昇している。また、ナフサ輸入に占
める中東の割合は 2026年 5月に 10％まで低下した後、7月以降は 50％前後まで持ち直している。
ただし、2025年の 73.8％と比べれば依然として低い水準にある。

価格面では、ドバイ原油の市況価格は 3月に 1バレル 130ドル近く（月中平均）まで急上昇した
後、6、7月には 80ドル前後まで低下した。その
後、8月に 80ドル台後半、9、10月には 110ドル
程度まで急上昇したが、11月には 100ドルを割り
込んでいる。一方、通関（入着）ベースの原油価
格は、市況価格の変動が遅れて反映されるが、3
月の 1バレル 60ドル台から 6月に 117ドルまで急
上昇した後、7月も 115ドルと高止まりした。8
月には 102ドル、9月には 101ドルまで低下した
ものの、市況価格が 6、7月に大きく低下したに
もかかわらず、通関価格は 9月まで 100ドルを上
回る高水準が続いている。 
原油の輸入量が前年の水準を回復する中、輸入価格が大幅に上昇したため、原油の輸入金額は 6
月に前年比で増加に転じた後、高い伸びが続いており、貿易赤字の拡大要因となっている。 
もともと、通関ベースの原油価格は、指標価格に調整金、タンカー運賃、保険料などが上乗せさ
れているが、中東情勢緊迫後は調整金、タンカー運賃が大幅に上昇したこと、代替調達先の米国か
らの輸入価格が中東を上回っていることなどから、市況価格との差が大きく拡大している。代替調
達が進展するもとでは、原油の輸入価格が市況価格ほど低下せずに貿易収支の悪化につながること
が見込まれる。

（AI関連財が輸出を牽引、交易条件の悪化を緩和） 
グローバルな AI関連需要の拡大を背景に、日本の輸出も AI関連財が牽引役となっている。財
務省の「貿易統計」をもとに、AI関連財1とそれ以外の財に分けて輸出動向を確認すると、輸出数
量全体はそれほど強くないものの、AI関連財は 2026年入り後、前年比二桁の高い伸びが続いてい
る。2026年 7-9月期の輸出数量は、AI関連財が前年比 13.7％と大きく増加した一方、非 AI関連財
は同 1.2％の小幅な増加にとどまった。AI関連財が輸出数量全体を下支えする構図となっている。 
AI関連財は数量だけでなく、価格の上昇も顕著となっている。世界的な AI投資の拡大に伴い、
半導体や電子部品などの需給の引き締まりがその背景にあると考えられる。2026年 7-9月期の AI
関連財の輸出価格は前年比 24.3％と大幅に上昇し、輸出数量の増加と合わせて輸出金額は同 38.0％

1 AI関連財は、WTOが公表する AI関連財（AI-Enabling-Products）103品目。2025年の輸出全体に占める AI関連財の割
合は 17.1％。

＜非AI関連財＞ ＜AI関連財＞

こうした AI関連財を中心とする輸出価格の上昇は、中東情勢の緊迫化に伴う交易条件の悪化を
緩和する役割も果たしている。原油をはじめとする資源価格の上昇は、輸入物価の上昇を通じて交
易条件を悪化させるが、今回は輸出物価も大幅に上昇している。このため、輸出物価を輸入物価で
除して求めた交易条件指数の悪化は比較的小幅にとどまっている。 
過去の資源価格高騰局面と比較すると、2005～2008年頃の世界的な資源価格高騰局面には、原
油などの輸入物価が大幅に上昇する一方、輸出物価の上昇がそれに追いつかず、交易条件が悪化し
た。また、2020年以降も、コロナ禍からの世界経済の回復に伴う資源価格の上昇に加え、2022年
のロシアによるウクライナ侵攻を受けた資源価格の急騰や円安の進行によって、交易条件が大幅に
悪化した。両局面ともに交易条件の累積的な悪化幅は▲30％以上となった。これに対し、2026年
1-3月期を起点とした 7-9月期までの 2四半期の悪化幅は▲6.6％である。AI関連財を中心とする輸
出価格の上昇が、原油価格の高騰に伴う輸入物価上昇の影響を一部相殺し、交易条件の悪化を緩和
している点が、過去の資源価格高騰局面との大き
な違いである。 
ただし、輸入物価の上昇率は輸出物価の上昇
率を上回っており、交易条件の悪化が続いている
ことに変わりはない。今後も原油価格が高止まり
する場合には、海外への所得流出を通じて国内経
済を下押しする可能性がある。一方、AI関連需
要の拡大を背景とした輸出価格の上昇が続けば、
交易条件の悪化を和らげる要因となるだろう。

（2027年の春闘賃上げ率は前年を若干上回る見込み） 
名目賃金を消費者物価（持家の帰属家賃を除く総合）で割り引いた実質賃金は、2026年 1月から
9ヵ月連続でプラスの伸びを維持している。名目賃金（現金給与総額）が 2025年中の 2％台前半か
ら 2026年入り後には 3％台へ伸びを高めたことに加え、政府によるエネルギー負担緩和策の影響な
どから、物価上昇率が大きく縮小したことが実質賃金の押し上げに寄与している。

消費者物価（持家の帰属家賃を除く総合）

2027年の春闘を取り巻く環境を確認すると、有効求人倍率が 1倍を上回り、失業率が 2％台半ば
で推移するなど、労働需給は引き締まった状態が続いており、企業収益は中東情勢緊迫化の影響を
受けながらも過去最高水準の更新を続けている。消費者物価は、政府によるエネルギー負担緩和策
の影響で 2026年入り後 1％台の伸びが続いていたが、9月に 9ヵ月ぶりの 2％台となり、今後上昇
ペースが加速することが確実となっている。 
賃上げの環境を過去と比較するために、労働需給（有効求人倍率）、企業収益（売上高経常利益
率）、物価（消費者物価上昇率（除く生鮮食品））について、過去平均（1985年～）からの乖離幅を
標準偏差で基準化してみると、3指標の合計は物価上昇率の鈍化を主因として前年を若干下回って
いるものの、企業収益のプラス幅が大きく拡大していることから、全体としては引き続き高水準と
なっている。賃上げの環境は引き続き良好と判断される。

2. 実質成長率は 2026年度 0.9％、2027年度 1.3％、2028年度 1.1％を予想

（2026年度後半は低成長が続く見通し） 
2026年 7-9月期のマイナス成長の主因となった民間消費の減少は、天候不順による一時的な影響
が大きい。ただし、こうした影響が解消されても、物価上昇の加速が消費の回復を妨げる可能性が
高い。消費者物価上昇率は 2026年 9月に 9ヵ月ぶりの 2％台となったが、原材料価格の上昇を耐久
財や日用品などの販売価格に転嫁する動きが今後さらに広がることから、2026年度末にかけて 3％
台まで高まる公算が大きい。物価上昇率の高まりによる実質購買力の低下を背景に 2026年度後半
の民間消費は弱めの動きが続くことが予想される。 
現時点では、2026年 10-12月期はプラス成長に復帰するものの、民間消費が伸び悩むことなどか
ら、前期比年率 0.6％とマイナス成長の後としては低めの伸びにとどまると予想している。2027年
1-3月期は、物価上昇率が 3％台まで高まることに加え、飲食料品の消費税率引き下げ前の買い控
えの影響もあり、民間消費は前期比でマイナスとなる可能性が高く、実質 GDP成長率は前期比年
率 0.2％とほぼゼロ成長にとどまるだろう。2026年度後半の日本経済は物価高による民間消費の低
迷を主因として、低空飛行が続くことが予想される。

予測

（消費税率引き下げの影響） 
政府は 2027年 4月から 2年間に限り、飲食料品（酒類、外食を除く）の消費税率を現在の 8％か
ら 1％に引き下げるとともに、1％相当の範囲内で中低所得者向けの給付を先行導入する予定として
いる。 
食料品の消費税率を 8％から 1％に引き下げると、家計の負担は年間 4.3兆円程度軽減される。こ
れにより期待されるのは、物価の低下、個人消費、実質 GDPの押し上げである。ニッセイ基礎研
究所のマクロモデルによる機械的な試算によれば、食料品の消費税率を 8％から 1％に引き下げた
場合、消費者物価（総合）は▲1.4％低下し、個人消費は 0.7％、実質 GDPは 0.5％押し上げられる。 
ただし、これは消費税率の引き下げ分（7％）が全て値下げに反映された場合の試算である。こ
れまで日本では消費税率引き上げ時にほぼ 100％価格転嫁されていたが、引き下げ時にそれが当て

（家計の実質可処分所得は依然としてコロナ禍前を下回る） 
2026年度入り後の家計消費の低迷は、高校授業料や小学校給食費の無償化による政府消費への振
り替え、豪雨・長雨などの天候不順の影響
もあるが、より長い目でみれば、実質可処
分所得の伸び悩みが消費低迷の主因である。
家計の実質可処分所得（季節調整値）はこ
のところ持ち直しているが、依然としてコ
ロナ禍前（2019年平均）の水準を若干下回
っている。

### Page 9

（恒常的な所得に連動する家計消費） 
実質家計消費と実質可処分所得は、平常時には連動性が非常に高い。しかし、コロナ禍で一人当
たり 10万円の特別定額給付金が支給された際は、厳しい行動制限が課せられていたことから貯蓄
率が急上昇し、両者が大きく乖離した。また、2024年に所得税・住民税減税が行われた際も可処分
所得の増加は消費の押し上げにほとんど寄与しなかった。 
このことは、消費の持続的な回復を実現するためには、一時的な所得の増加ではなく、恒常的な
所得の増加が重要であることを示している。 
実質可処分所得は、名目賃金の高い伸びが続く一方、物価の上昇ペースが加速することから、2026
年度末にかけて低迷し、消費も停滞することが予想される。2027年度に入ると、食料品の消費税率
引き下げに伴う物価上昇率の低下によって実質
可処分所得が押し上げられ、消費も伸びを高め
るだろう。ただし、消費税率の引き下げが 2年
間の時限措置となっているため、実質所得の増
加のかなりの部分は貯蓄に回ることが想定され
る。このため、実質可処分所得の増加に比べて
家計消費の増加は小幅にとどまる公算が大きい。
家計貯蓄率は足もとの 1％程度から 2027年度に
は 2％台まで上昇するだろう。 
民間消費は 2026年度が前年比 0.0％、2027年
度が同 1.4％、2028年度が同 0.9％と予想する。

（設備投資は回復するが、供給制約が抑制要因に） 
2026年 7-9月期の設備投資は前期比 1.6％の高い伸びとなったが、4-6月期に特許権の海外譲渡に
伴う研究開発投資の減少によって落ち込んだ反動によって押し上げられた面がある。設備投資は回
復しているものの、企業収益が過去最高の更新を続けていることを考慮すれば、そのペースは緩や
かなものにとどまっている。 
日銀短観などの設備投資計画の強さに対し、GDP統計の設備投資が弱く見える理由のひとつは、
設備投資計画は名目（金額）で、GDP統計の設備投資は設備投資デフレーターで割り引いた実質で
みることが多いことである。名目設備投資は 2020年 4-6月期の 94.1兆円（季節調整済・年率換算
値）を底に着実に増加し、2026年 7-9月期には 135.4兆円となり、この間の伸びは 43.8％となって

### Page 10

また、企業の設備投資意欲は強いものの、人手不足などの供給制約により工事の進捗が遅れてい
ることも設備投資の抑制要因となっている可能性がある。機械受注残高と建設工事の手持ち工事高
を、それぞれ販売額、施工高の何ヵ月分に相当するかでみると、いずれも長期的に増加傾向にある。
高水準の受注残高は先行きの設備投資拡大を示唆すると同時に、人手不足などの供給制約により機
械の生産や建設工事が進まず、計画に比べて実際の設備投資の進捗が遅れている可能性も示してい
る。

設備投資は、高水準の企業収益を背景に、人手不足対応の省力化投資、デジタル化に向けた情報
関連投資、AI・半導体関連等の成長分野への投資、Eコマース拡大に伴う建設投資などの案件を軸
に、先行きも増加傾向が続くことが予想される。ただし、資材価格の高騰や人手不足による工事の
遅れから、企業収益の高い伸びや設備投資意欲の強さに比べればそのペースは緩やかにとどまる可
能性が高い。 
設備投資は 2025年度の前年比 2.2％から 2026年度に同 1.0％と減速した後、2027年度が同 2.8％、
2028年度が同 2.7％と伸びを高めると予想する。

（物価の見通し） 
消費者物価（生鮮食品を除く総合、以下コア CPI）は、2026年 1月に前年比 1.9％と 2022年 3月
以来、3年 10ヵ月ぶりの 2％割れとなった後、1％
台の伸びが続いていたが、9月には同 2.1％と 9
ヵ月ぶりの 2％台となった。 
コア CPI上昇率が 1％台で推移していたのは、
主として補助金政策によって物価上昇率が押し
下げられているためである。ガソリンや電気・
ガス代等の負担緩和策、教育無償化政策などの
特殊要因は 2025年 12月以降、物価上昇率の押
し下げ要因となっており、2026年 9月の押し下

### Page 11

食料（生鮮食品を除く）は 2025年 7月の前年比 8.3％をピークに鈍化傾向が続き、2026年 8月に
同 2.7％となった後、9月には同 2.9％と 14ヵ月ぶりに上昇率が拡大した。帝国データバンクの「食
品主要 195社価格動向調査」（10/30公表）によれば、2026年（1～12月）の飲食料品の値上げ品目
数は 2万 327品目となり、前年同時期の 2万
580品目を若干下回っている。中東情勢悪化
に伴う原材料価格の高騰を受けて、先行き 3
ヵ月の値上げ品目数は 2026年 6月以降、前年
比で増加に転じているが、そのペースはやや
鈍化している。消費者物価の食料（生鮮食品
を除く）は、上昇率が高まるものの、米価格
の下落が当面続くこともあり、2025年夏場に
比べればそのペースは緩やかにとどまること
が見込まれる。 
一方、エネルギー価格は、政府による負担緩和策によって抑制されてきたが、電気・ガス料金へ
の支援策は 2026年 9月使用分（消費者物価への反映は 10月）でいったん終了した。2026年 12月
使用分からは支援策が再開されるものの、補助金額が前年を若干下回ることに加え、2026年春以降
の原油価格高騰の影響が燃料費調整単価に遅れて反映されることから、今冬の電気・ガス料金は前
年を大きく上回ることが見込まれる。さらに、政府は、ガソリン価格の高騰を抑制するため、2026
年 3月から 11月まで、補助金によってレギュラーガソリンの全国平均小売価格を 1リットル当た
り 170円程度に抑えてきたが、12月以降は補助金を段階的に縮小し、価格抑制の基準価格を毎月 5
円ずつ引き上げる方針を示した。このため、エネルギー価格は 2026年 9月に前年比▲0.6％から上
昇ペースが急加速し、2027年入り後には前年比で二桁の伸びとなり、コア CPI上昇率への寄与度は
1％を超えるだろう。 
今回の見通しでは、電気・ガス料金への支
援策については、2027年夏以降も補助金を段
階的に縮小しながら継続し、ガソリン補助金
については、価格抑制の基準価格を 2027年 2
月に 1リットル当たり 185円まで段階的に引
き上げた後、同水準で据え置くことを前提と
している。

台新證券投資顧問股份有限公司 台北市仁愛路四段 118 號 16 樓(02)5589-9558  (113)金管投顧新字第 021 號 
公司拜訪快報

聯強(2347)：資金成本上揚壓抑短期營收動能，但長期營益率改善趨勢
不變 
建議：維持買進

一、聯強為亞太地區最大 3C 通
路商，營收成長以半導體業務最
佳  
 聯強為 3C 電子產品之銷售、配送、維修、量身訂做即時生產之專業通路商，為
亞太地區 3C 通路商龍頭，2026 年前三季產品比重分別為商用加值佔 31%、資
訊消費佔 17% 、 半 導 體 佔 48% 、 手 機 產 品 佔 4% ，成長主要來商用加值
(+112%YoY)和半導體業務(+162%YoY)。聯強海外市場包含中國、香港、紐澳、
泰國、印尼、印度及越南等地，其中以中國、台灣和印尼等區域較佳，營收分別
為 YoY+109%、YoY+78%和 YoY+18%。聯強競爭優勢為透過其四合一經營模
式，搭配其綿密的通路發揮規模經濟，並提供高附加價值的後勤運籌(庫存管理
與快速維為修服務等)，於中國主要競爭對手為神州數碼、天音通信控股。

二、半導體業務成長性佳，3Q26 
EPS 2.93 元創新高並優於預期 
 3Q26 營收 2366 億元(+18%QoQ；+136%YoY)優於預估值 14%，其中商用加
值和半導體營收分別為+116%YOY 和+273%YoY，因秉棄部分利潤不佳專案，
毛利率 3.49%優於前季之 3.4%，營業利益 61.41 億元(+28%%QoQ)優於預估
值 24%，因營運資金需求以及籌資成本上升致使利息費用提高至 9.34 億元
(+106%QoQ)，稅後純益 48.83 億元(+25%QoQ；+90%YoY)，EPS 2.93 元優
於預期之 2.5 元並創新高。

三、資金成本上揚導致業務拓展
較為謹慎，估計 4Q26 EPS 2.34
元 
 由於資金成本上揚致使公司放棄部分利潤不佳半導體業務，十月營收月減 34%，
估計 4Q26 營收 2046 億元(-14%QoQ；YoY+62%)，因產品組合變化轉佳，毛
利率提升至 3.58%，稅後純益 38.99 億元(-20%QoQ；+41%YoY)，EPS 2.34
元。

四、AI 長線需求有助商用加值和
半導體業務成長性 
 集邦 預估 2026 和 2027 年全 球九 大 CSP 資 本支 出分 別為 0.95 兆 美元
(+106%YoY)和 1.53 兆美元(+60%YoY)，且 AI 需求從雲端擴展到企業端，其中
以商用加值和半導體業務受惠幅度最大。 
在商用加值業務方面，在台灣和中國資料中心產品出貨挹注下，3Q26 營收
+116%YoY。展望 2027 年，中國十十五規劃和國產化政策推動政府和國企加速
採用本土品牌，推升聯強代理華為、浪潮等品牌業務成長性，台灣則是接獲數個
半導體大廠 AI 基礎建設專案，加計中小企業 AI 建置需求逐步顯現，商用業務將
為營運成長主要動能之一。 
在半導體業務方面，缺貨和漲價效致使記憶體、SSD 和硬碟業務為半導體業務
中 成 長 性 最 佳 者 ， 且 AI 推 論 亦 增 加 CPU 需求，3Q26 半 導 體 業 務 營 收

五、資金成本上揚將致使接案更
為謹慎，但長期營益率好轉方向
不變 
 聯強今年接獲數個大型 AI 基礎建設專案，致使營收動能以及營運資金需求大增，
3Q26 遇到資金成本上揚，致使利息費用較前季倍增，公司將透過聯貸、可轉債
以及現增降低利息負擔，預期三項籌資工具皆完成後可望降低利息費用 20-
30%。展望 2027 年，儘管營收成長性不如 2026 年亮眼，但接單策略轉趨謹慎
將致使毛利率下滑壓力降低，且預期營業費用增幅有限下，營益率持續好轉方向
不變。

六、維持買進理由  1)預估 2026 和 2027 年 EPS 分別為 9.38 元和 11.07 元。 
2)儘管資金成本上揚壓抑短期營收動能，但長期營益率改善趨勢不變，維持買進
評等，TP 100 元 (2027 PE x 9)。

2382 廣達電腦(Quanta)

報告日期：2026/11/12 研究員：曾智楷(Kevin Tseng) 
壹、訪 談／法 說 內 容 
重點摘要： 
 3Q26 營收 1 兆 1155 億，YoY +125%，毛利率 4.75%，稅後
淨利 284 億，稅後 EPS 7.08 元。 
 4Q26 新世代 GPU 平台 AI 機櫃預計將進入小批量量產，通用
型伺服器出貨量亦將持續成長，而筆電第四季出貨量則預計將
呈雙位數季減。 
 公司預估 2027 年，伺服器佔總營收比重將進一步攀升至
85%~90%，其中 AI 伺服器佔比將突破 80%。筆電則受記憶體
價格影響，預估營收佔比將降至個位數百分比。

一. 3Q26 毛利率 4.75%，EPS 7.08 元 
廣達 3Q26 營收 1 兆 1155 億，QoQ +7.6% ，YoY +125.2% ，產
品組合部分，受惠於 AI 機櫃 ASP 持續走高，第三季 AI 伺服器營
收呈現季增，但出貨量則較上季略減，主要由於第三季處於新舊
產品世代的轉換期。通用伺服器受惠於 AI Agent 與資料中心需求
擴大，第三季通用型伺服器出貨量表現優於預期。而筆電部分，
第三季出貨量為 960 萬台，QoQ -17%，YoY -24%，主要由於記
憶體漲價影響，以至於今年下半年終端市場需求明顯疲弱。獲利
方面，3Q26 營業毛利 530.2 億，QoQ +1.8%，YoY +56.3%，毛
利率 4.75%，QoQ -0.27ppts，YoY -2.1ppts，公司表示，毛利率
下滑主因高單價 AI 機櫃出貨 ASP 大幅躍升產生稀釋效應，加
上筆電材料成本上揚與規模縮減所致，並非本業獲利能力惡化。
營業利益 322 億，QoQ -6.0% ，YoY +75.3% ，營利率 2.89%，
QoQ -0.41ppts ，YoY -0.82ppts 。3Q26 業外淨利 26.8 億，稅後
284.2 億，QoQ -0.8%，YoY +73.0%，單季稅後 EPS 7.08 元。

二. 4Q26 AI 伺服器動能延續，筆電持續面臨逆風 
公司公告 10 月營收 3532.5 億，MoM +8.6%，YoY +104%，今年
累計營收 3.31 兆，YoY +99.9%。展望 4Q26，公司表示，隨著新
世代 GPU 平台 AI 機櫃於第四季逐步進入小批量量產，受惠於新
產品 ASP 顯著拉升，第四季 AI 伺服器營收仍將維持強勁季增。
通用型伺服器第四季出貨量亦預計持續成長，而筆電第四季出貨
量則預期呈雙位數季減。另外，毛利率部分，由於高單價 AI 機櫃
出貨佔比提升持續稀釋毛利率，再加上筆電出貨規模縮減，預期
4Q26 毛利率將較第三季下滑。預估 4Q26 營收 1.15 兆，QoQ 
+3.8%，YoY +81.3%，毛 利率 4.7%，營利率 2.9%，稅後淨利 290

三. 預估 2026/2027 年 EPS 26.75/31.86 元 
展望 2026 年，公司預估，2026 全年伺服器佔比將達 80%~85%。
其中 AI 伺服器表現優於原先倍增目標，佔整體伺服器營收比重約
75%~80%，佔今年全年營收約 60%~65%。通用型伺服器亦優於
預期，呈雙位數年增，而筆電部分，則受到終端需求疲弱影響，
全年出貨量預計年減 15%~20%。預估公司 2026 全年營收 4.12
兆，YoY +94% ，毛利率 4.81%，營利率 2.99%，稅後淨利 1073
億，全年稅後 EPS 26.75 元。展望 2027 年，隨著新世代 AI 機櫃
於 1Q27 放量，ASP 提升約 50%至接近翻倍，AMD 專案亦將持續
出貨，再加上 ASIC 業務預計明年將有雙位數的成長，預估 2027
年 AI 伺服器營收將維持高雙位數年增；通用型伺服器 2027 年出
貨量與營收亦將維持雙位數增長。預估 2027 年伺服器佔總營收比
重將進一步攀升至 85%~90%，其中 AI 伺服器佔比將突破 80%。
而筆電則受記憶體價格居高不下影響，預估 2027 年出貨量將年減
高個位數至 10%，營收佔比將降至個位數百分比。資本支出方面，
今年前三季資本支出為 346 億元，公司先前宣布終止友達華亞廠
收購案，將轉向擴大海外佈局，預估 2027 年資本支出將略增至
400~450 億元，主要投入美國加州與泰國廠區，以支應未來 12~24
個月 CSP 客戶明確的產能預留需求。預估公司 2027 全年營收 5.85
兆，YoY +42% ，毛利率 4.37%，營利率 2.79%，稅後淨利 1278
億，全年稅後 EPS 31.86 元。

四. 投資建議為「買進」，目標價 385 元 
受惠於 AI 伺服器的強勁需求，公司預估明年 AI 伺服器營收將雙
位數成長，且隨美系四大 CSP 資本支出展望維持正向，GPU、ASIC
伺服器專案持續放量，將有望挹注公司營運持續增長。以公司 2027
年 EPS 來看，目前股價交易於 9~10 倍本益比，評價仍具上修空
間，給予買進投資建議，目標價為 385 元(12x 2027 EPS)。

貳、產 業 及 營 運 概 況

一.公司簡介 
廣達電腦(Quanta)為全球筆記型電腦專業研發設計製造與雲端運
算解決方案領導供應商，為全球前五大筆記型電腦代工廠商。公
司主要替品牌業者從事代工服務，代工項目包含筆記型電腦、平
板電腦、智慧型手機、伺服器、商用及家用智慧物聯網裝置、消
費性電子產品、車用電子等。公司總部位於桃園龜山，生產基地
包含桃園、大陸上海、重慶、江蘇、美國田納西州、加州、墨西

二.產業趨勢概況 
1. 明年 CSP Capex 預估破 1.5 兆美元，AI 伺服器產值年增逾 9 成 
根據集邦科技(TrendForce)最新研究，受惠於 Google、Amazon、
Meta、Microsoft、Oracle 及中國四大科技巨頭等全球前九大雲端
服務供應商(CSP)擴大投資 AI 基礎設施，預估 2026 年九大 CSP
資本支出總額將逾 9,570 億美元，YoY +106%，2027 年更因伺服
器整機漲價、零組件缺貨漲價及供應商要求預付款等因素，有望
突破 1.5 兆美元，YoY +60%，雙雙優於預期。在超大型 CSP、Tier-2
資料中心與主權 AI 等需求帶動下，預估 2026 年 AI 伺服器產值約
4,790 億美元，占整體伺服器產值近 75%；2027 年隨輝達 GB300、
VR200 與超微 Helios 等整櫃式方案成為主流，加上記憶體等關鍵
零組件短缺推升單價，預計整體伺服器產值將首度跨越 1 兆美元，
其中 AI 伺服器產值將躍升至近 9,200 億美元 YoY +90%，占比進
一步提高至 81%左右。

圖二、2024-2027 年九大 CSP 資本支出總額預估(單位:十億美元)

資料來源：TrendForce、宏遠投顧整理

2. 2026 年全球 AI 伺服器出貨量預計年增 31% 
根據 TrendForce 最新 AI server 產業研究，近期超大型 CSP、
Tier-2 資料中心業者對 NVIDIA GB/VR 等整櫃式 AI server 方案的
採購意願明顯提高，且 Google、AWS 新一代 ASIC 平台於 2026
下半年陸續放量，加上中系業者擴大採用本地 AI 方案以滿足雲端
大型語言模型(LLM) AI 需求，促使 TrendForce 上調 2026 年 AI 
server 出貨年成長幅度，從原先的 28%更新為近 31%。北美五大
業者 2026 年的資本支出多翻倍提升，反映出大型 CSP 廠持續擴
建 AI 資料中心、GPU 叢集、液冷散熱等新一代基礎設施，以應對
生成式 AI 及大型模型快速成長帶來的算力需求。其中，Google 
2026 至 2027 年都將側重自家 TPU 晶片發展，2027 年出貨量有
望繼續高速成長。AWS 2026 年以 NVIDIA GB300 作為 GPU AI 
server 主力，自研 ASIC 也穩定出貨，預期至 2027 年量能將再擴
大。Meta 2026 年主要採用 NVIDIA GB/VR、AMD Helios 整櫃式
方案，自研 ASIC 布局預計至 2027 年將明顯加速，為該公司長期
降低 AI 基礎設施成本、提升推論效率的重要策略。

叁、業 績 及 獲 利 預 估

一. 4Q26 AI 伺服器動能延續，筆電持續面臨逆風 
公司公告 10 月營收 3532.5 億，MoM +8.6%，YoY +104%，今年
累計營收 3.31 兆，YoY +99.9%。展望 4Q26，公司表示，隨著新
世代 GPU 平台 AI 機櫃於第四季逐步進入小批量量產，受惠於新
產品 ASP 顯著拉升，第四季 AI 伺服器營收仍將維持強勁季增。
通用型伺服器第四季出貨量亦預計持續成長，而筆電第四季出貨
量則預期呈雙位數季減。另外，毛利率部分，由於高單價 AI 機櫃
出貨佔比提升持續稀釋毛利率，再加上筆電出貨規模縮減，預期

4Q26 毛利率將較第三季下滑。預估 4Q26 營收 1.15 兆，QoQ 
+3.8%，YoY +81.3%，毛 利率 4.7%，營利率 2.9%，稅後淨利 290
億元，單季稅後 EPS 7.25 元。

二. 預估 2026/2027 年 EPS 26.75/31.86 元 
展望 2026 年，公司預估，2026 全年伺服器佔比將達 80%~85%。
其中 AI 伺服器表現優於原先倍增目標，佔整體伺服器營收比重約
75%~80%，佔今年全年營收約 60%~65%。通用型伺服器亦優於
預期，呈雙位數年增，而筆電部分，則受到終端需求疲弱影響，
全年出貨量預計年減 15%~20%。預估公司 2026 全年營收 4.12
兆，YoY +94% ，毛利率 4.81%，營利率 2.99%，稅後淨利 1073
億，全年稅後 EPS 26.75 元。展望 2027 年，隨著新世代 AI 機櫃
於 1Q27 放量，ASP 提升約 50%至接近翻倍，AMD 專案亦將持續
出貨，再加上 ASIC 業務預計明年將有雙位數的成長，預估 2027
年 AI 伺服器營收將維持高雙位數年增；通用型伺服器 2027 年出
貨量與營收亦將維持雙位數增長。預估 2027 年伺服器佔總營收比
重將進一步攀升至 85%~90%，其中 AI 伺服器佔比將突破 80%。
而筆電則受記憶體價格居高不下影響，預估 2027 年出貨量將年減
高個位數至 10%，營收佔比將降至個位數百分比。資本支出方面，
今年前三季資本支出為 346 億元，公司先前宣布終止友達華亞廠
收購案，將轉向擴大海外佈局，預估 2027 年資本支出將略增至
400~450 億元，主要投入美國加州與泰國廠區，以支應未來 12~24
個月 CSP 客戶明確的產能預留需求。預估公司 2027 全年營收 5.85
兆，YoY +42% ，毛利率 4.37%，營利率 2.79%，稅後淨利 1278
億，全年稅後 EPS 31.86 元。

Greater China Technology Hardware
Asia Pacific
Industry View In-Line
4Q NB build forecast cut 5% despite October’s beat: October ODM NB shipments
came in 4% above MSe, but we lower our 4Q26 build forecast by 5% to 21.9M units
(-13% q/q, -32% y/y) to reflect the latest ODM guidance. The revised forecast implies

limited quarter-end pull-in strength and a deeper y/y contraction than the 26%
decline in 3Q26. Despite an already depressed 3Q base, the projected 13%
sequential decline is well below the 15-year average of flat q/q shipments in 4Q.
PC outlook seen continuing to weaken into 2027: NB build trends have weakened
over recent months, while increasingly cautious ODM commentary provides further
evidence of pressure on PC demand. Asustek expects global PC unit shipments to

decline 10–15% y/y in 2027 (link), while Quanta expects global NB shipments to
drop by high single digits to 10% y/y (link). We believe 2027 outlooks from the
supply chain have worsened in recent months, and both outlooks are directionally
consistent with our forecast for an 11.5% y/y decline in global PC units in 2027.
Looking to 1Q27, our preliminary view is a further sequential decline in NB builds,
although we have yet to establish a formal forecast.

Key Takeaways
Top five ODMs' October 2026 NB shipments were 7.1M units (-27% m/m, -30% y/
y), 4% above our estimate.
We see November 2026 NB builds at 7.3M units (+4% m/m, -30% y/y).
We lower our 4Q26 NB build estimate by 5% to 21.9M units (-13% q/q, -32% y/y).
Our top five ODMs' CY26 NB build estimate is ~106.6M units (-17% y/y).

Hold or Sell to the stocks we cover. Overweight, Equal-weight, Not-Rated and Underweight are not the equivalent of buy, hold and sell.  Investors should carefully read the definitions of all
ratings used in Morgan Stanley Research. In addition, since Morgan Stanley Research contains more complete information concerning the analyst's views, investors should carefully read Morgan

Stanley Research, in its entirety, and not infer the contents from the rating alone.  In any case, ratings (or research) should not be used or relied upon as investment advice.  An investor's decision
to buy or sell a stock should depend on individual circumstances (such as the investor's existing holdings) and other considerations.

Analyst Stock Ratings
Overweight (O). The stock's total return is expected to exceed the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next
12-18 months.
Equal-weight (E). The stock's total return is expected to be in line with the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over

the next 12-18 months.
Not-Rated (NR). Currently the analyst does not have adequate conviction about the stock's total return relative to the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage
universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next 12-18 months.
Underweight (U). The stock's total return is expected to be below the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next

12-18 months.
Unless otherwise specified, the time frame for price targets included in Morgan Stanley Research is 12 to 18 months.
Analyst Industry Views
Attractive (A): The analyst expects the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months to be attractive vs. the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated
below.
In-Line (I): The analyst expects the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months to be in line with the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.

Cautious (C): The analyst views the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months with caution vs. the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.
Benchmarks for each region are as follows: North America - S&P 500; Latin America - relevant MSCI country index or MSCI Latin America Index; Europe - MSCI Europe; Japan - TOPIX; Asia -
relevant MSCI country index or MSCI sub-regional index or MSCI AC Asia Pacific ex Japan Index.

### Page 1

M
Idea
Delta Electronics Inc.  | Asia Pacific
Momentum to Pick Up in
Coming Quarters
We expect revenue to grow in  double digits (%) QoQ in both
4Q26 and 1Q27.  Low market expectations for HVDC in the 2H27
GPU platform skew risk/reward more  to the upside.
Revenue momentum picking up with better component supply: With October
monthly sales of NT$$74,666mn (+14% MoM/+30% YoY), we think Delta is on
tracking for 10-15% top-line growth QoQ for 4Q26, as component supply increases

and some power rack shipments  emerge, albeit with a relatively small contribution.
We expect the momentum to be sustained into 1Q27, likely another double-digit (%)
QoQ rise on the top line, along with the new GPU server ramp and postponed
shipments from past quarters. We anticipate  strong revenue growth in 2027, as
power consumption will increase significantly in the new GPU server racks. The
contributions from liquid cooling solutions and DC/DC modules will also be good

drivers.
HVDC power rack timing key to watch, but expectations not high: We expect
Delta to start shipping HVDC power racks to its customers from 4Q26, though the
contribution might be a low-single-digit (%) share of total revenue through 1H27, by
our estimate. All eyes are on the adoption of HVDC power racks in the next-
generation AI server platforms – we think power consumption at the rack level is at
the threshold of HVDC power racks being a necessity. Our checks indicate that most

supply chain players now see HVDC as the reference design for the GPU server
generation scheduled for 2H27. Though this does not appear finalized yet, the
chances of that happening should increase  moving closer to the launch. Moreover,
we believe that the base case for most people is for HVDC not to be the only power
delivery structure in that 2H27 GPU server platform, which points to potential
upside in 2H27/2028.
Liquid cooling and fuel cell good complementary businesses: Delta has been a

dominant supplier in liquid cooling solutions, which should show adoption
proliferating from computing/switch trays to more devices, including power shelves,
optical transceivers, etc. For the fuel cell business, we believe Delta's expertise in
power delivery could help it offer more integrated solutions. Smaller-scale projects
should start from 2027 and could grow into a much more meaningful revenue
stream. The board  approved a greenfield project in Guanyin (Taiwan) with  capex of

~NT$10bn back in May 2026. We expect fuel cells to reach a multi-billion dollar (US
$) revenue scale in the coming few years. .
Stay OW: Current valuation is 16x 2027e P/E vs. the trough level of 14x. We view
risk-reward as attractive, given Delta's dominant position in AI power/cooling related
offerings. We stay OW on the stock and believe that accelerating momentum in the
near term should help it outperform. Our NT$2,300 price target is based on 30x

Morgan Stanley appreciates your support in
the 2027 Extel Asia Research Survey.
Request your ballot                          here.

### Page 4

M
Idea
4
Valuation Methodology
Our price target (base case scenario value) is NT$2,300, derived from 30x average
2027-2028e EPS: We use price/earnings as our valuation methodology because we
believe it appropriately captures Delta's earnings growth potential. We believe our target
P/E multiple of 30x is justified by an earnings CAGR of 59% over 2025-28e, which implies
a PEG of 0.5. This is lower than the average of 1.0 in 2022-25, when the stock was trading

at an average P/E of 23x for a 23% earnings CAGR.
Bull case scenario value – NT$3,050: We apply a 40x P/E multiple   to our average base
case estimate for 2027-28. This scenario assumes a greater-than-expected contribution
from higher-margin businesses such as power electronics and liquid cooling as well as
greater synergies from acquired companies.
Bear case scenario value – NT$920 (from NT$1,050):  We apply a 12x P/E multiple  (from
14x) to our average base case estimate for 2027-28. This scenario assumes further profit

drag from legacy products, smaller profit contribution from higher-margin businesses,
higher opex and/or less meaningful contribution from acquired companies.

Analyst Stock Ratings
Overweight (O). The stock's total return is expected to exceed the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next
12-18 months.
Equal-weight (E). The stock's total return is expected to be in line with the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over

the next 12-18 months.
Not-Rated (NR). Currently the analyst does not have adequate conviction about the stock's total return relative to the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage
universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next 12-18 months.
Underweight (U). The stock's total return is expected to be below the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next

12-18 months.
Unless otherwise specified, the time frame for price targets included in Morgan Stanley Research is 12 to 18 months.
Analyst Industry Views
Attractive (A): The analyst expects the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months to be attractive vs. the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated
below.
In-Line (I): The analyst expects the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months to be in line with the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.

Cautious (C): The analyst views the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months with caution vs. the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.
Benchmarks for each region are as follows: North America - S&P 500; Latin America - relevant MSCI country index or MSCI Latin America Index; Europe - MSCI Europe; Japan - TOPIX; Asia -
relevant MSCI country index or MSCI sub-regional index or MSCI AC Asia Pacific ex Japan Index.

大銀微系統(4576.TT) 
產業：電機機械 
受惠半導體設備擴充潮，2027 年營收成⻑將⾼於 20%  
 投資評價與建議 
高毛利率的奈微米定位平台營收比重已近60%，產品結構及附加價值提升帶動
毛利率提升，技術升級、集團分工與產能區域化佈局將帶動大銀微2027年營收
成⻑高於20%。 
 重點評論分析 
3Q26營收年增77%，4Q26營收可望與3Q26持平或略⾼：亞洲(台灣除外)、
台灣、美洲及歐洲地區3Q26分別年增89%、101%、127%及-12%，全球半導
體供應鏈重組與在地化採購需求強勁，3Q26營收12.2億元季增19%(年增
77%)，高階定位平台出貨佔比提升及規模經濟發揮，毛利率42.4%季增
5.3pp(年增8.6pp)，規模經濟及費用控制得宜下，營業利益率24.3%季增
6.4pp(年增13.9pp)；稅前淨利3.3億元季增113%(年增255%)，EPS 2.15元，
1~3Q26EPS 4.07元；4Q26營收可望與3Q26持平或略高，毛利率將維持在
40%上下。 
發展策略將持續朝向⾼附加價值市場，2027年營收成⻑將⾼於20%：未來的
發展策略將持續朝向高附加價值市場邁進，包括發展精密力量控制技術（涵蓋
先進封裝Hybrid Bonding、高速固晶機等應用），大銀微將專注於系統整合
銷售，不單獨販售E1控制器，以確保軟硬體深度綁定，帶動營收與獲利持續同
步雙位數成⻑，2027年營收成⻑高於20%。

| 營運概況 
3Q26毛利率及營益率均大增

資料來源 : CMoney 
3Q26營收年增77%，毛利率及營益率均大增：亞洲(台灣除外)、台灣、美洲及歐洲地區3Q26分別年增89%、101%、
127%及-12%，全球半導體供應鏈重組與在地化採購需求強勁，3Q26營收12.2億元季增19%(年增77%)，高階定位平台
出貨佔比提升及規模經濟發揮，毛利率42.4%季增5.3pp(年增8.6pp)，規模經濟及費用控制得宜下，營業利益率24.3%
季增6.4pp(年增13.9pp)；稅前淨利3.3億元季增113%(年增255%)，EPS 2.15元，1~3Q26EPS 4.07元；毛利率提升主
要是產品結構及附加價值提升，奈微米定位平台由於高度客製化、整合空氣軸承、花崗岩平台、多軸線性馬達與高階雷
射補償演算法，且直接對接半導體⼀線客戶，毛利率達40~50%；精密運動及控制元件過去單賣馬達線圈與磁軌毛利率
未及30%，但大銀微力推「馬達＋自製E1系列驅動器＋控制器」整套搭配銷售，加上高單價晶圓移載用直驅力矩馬達出
貨放量，使元件部門毛利率亦同步墊高至30~35%。 
4Q26營收可望與3Q26持平或略⾼，毛利率將維持在40%上下： 4Q26營收可望與3Q26持平或略高，毛利率將維持在
40%上下，目前高階平台訂單能見度達5~6個月，交期已縮短至3個月；元件類能見度約3個月，交期約2~2.5個月，大
銀微具備85%的關鍵零組件自製率（包含線性馬達、驅動器、控制器），能快速打樣並提供高彈性交期。 
發展策略將持續朝向⾼附加價值市場，2027年營收成⻑將⾼於20%：未來的發展策略將持續朝向高附加價值市場邁進，
包括發展精密力量控制技術（涵蓋先進封裝Hybrid Bonding、高速固晶機等應用），大銀微將專注於系統整合銷售，不
單獨販售E1控制器，以確保軟硬體深度綁定，帶動營收與獲利持續同步雙位數成⻑，2027年營收成⻑高於20%；隨著AI

晶片尺寸逼近光罩極限，晶圓代工龍頭與OSAT廠加速推進FOPLP與矩陣式大面積封裝，大面積玻璃或有機基板在高速
取放、曝光與AOI檢測時，傳統滾珠螺桿會產生背隙與熱變形，必須全面改採「大跨距龍門雙驅無鐵芯線性馬達平台」
與「大型花崗岩空氣軸承系統」，大銀微在此領域具備完整大尺寸平台調校經驗，單機採用金額較傳統封裝設備提升2~3
倍；環繞式閘極與晶背供電大幅增加製程道數與光學/電子束檢測站點，歐美設備客戶為提升毛利率，持續將次系統委由
大銀微ODM，訂單能見度已直達2Q27；全球AI伺服器主板與交換器板廠在泰國大舉擴產，帶動高階雷射鑽孔機、機械
鑽孔機與成型機對大銀微線性馬達與高階驅動器的強勁拉貨；配合上銀(2049)集團佈局AI人型機器人，大銀微的高轉矩
密度無框力矩馬達與微型驅動器，具備切入機器人旋轉關節與靈巧⼿電控系統之⻑期潛力。 
CPO 相關營收訂單能見度已達 2H27：AI 資料中心算力互連由銅線轉向光學互連（800G/1.6T 光模組與 CPO），光
纖陣列（FAU）與矽光晶片波導進行主動對位（Active Alignment）時，要求定位精度必須達到奈米級且兼具極高反
應速度與抗震性，大銀微系統能解決高倍率顯微鏡檢測時的微小震動干擾，有效提升客戶設備產出率，主要應用於光
學引擎測試與系統級檢測，優勢在於可提供高精密、輕薄且體積緊湊的六軸定位平台，E1 控制器能將伺服穩定度提升
至正負 1 奈米，由於大銀的線性馬達是完全自行研發，因此可做到比業界體積更小，且可做到機電整合；大銀微開發
之多自由度奈米定位平台已切入台系與美系矽光子檢測及封裝設備鏈，2Q26 起開始交貨國內矽光子檢測設備大廠，
2H26 進⼀步放量，訂單能見度已達 2H27。 
2027 年資本支出為 1.2~1.5 億元，將新增 20~30%產能：面對全球半導體供應鏈區域化（美、日、歐晶圓廠擴建）
以及 AI 伺服器帶動高階 PCB/載板廠大舉移往泰國與東南亞設廠，大銀微採取「核心奈米平台留台灣台中擴產、標準
控制元件與次系統模組強化亞洲及歐美前線組裝」的雙軌策略，2H26 台中廠區針對高階微奈米定位系統與精密運動元
件已啟動擴產計畫，擴產產能規劃於 4Q26~1Q27 陸續完成，屆時將增加 20~30%的產能，進⼀步⽀撐 4Q26~1Q27
的出貨順暢度；第⼀階段產能擴建已完成，第二階段擴產預計於 2027 年 2 月底至 3 月中旬完成，並已開始規劃第三
階段，2027 年資本⽀出為 1.2~1.5 億元。 
微奈米定位系統營收佔比逐步攀⾼

資料來源 : 大銀微，玉山投顧整理及預估、繪製

| 大銀微的定位與優勢 
大銀微掌握直驅馬達、線性馬達與驅動控制技術：大銀微系統成立於1997年，為上銀集團旗下的機電與超精密定位核心
企業，在集團分工體系中，⺟公司上銀（2049）負責提供高剛性機械結構與精密線性傳動部件，而大銀微則負責提供「大
腦（控制器）」、「神經（驅動器）」與「直接驅動心臟（線性馬達、力矩馬達、音圈馬達）」，大銀微與上銀為機電
整合雙引擎，大銀微掌握直驅馬達、線性馬達與驅動控制技術，而上銀專精於奈米與微米級的機械傳動（螺桿、線軌、
減速機）；大銀微的優勢包括馬達與驅動器與控制器「三合⼀」全自製能力(可針對奈米級平台進行底層演算法調校)、

### Page 4

4 
集團垂直整合綜效(結合上銀高精度螺桿/滑軌與自⾝線性馬達，提供⼀站式機電整合⽅案)、已通過全球前三大半導體設
備商及晶圓代工龍頭嚴苛認證。 
產業現狀：日本政府禁止高階工具機出口中國大陸，而中國大陸也管制高精度工具機出口，在全球貿易壁壘高築下，包
括北美、東南亞訂單大舉湧向台灣工具機業者；惟台灣工具機整機廠高階市場現面臨日本工具機廠挾日圓貶值優勢的威
脅，中低階或泛用型標準機又遭逢大陸業者低價搶市的威脅。 
產品應用範圍涵蓋半導體設備、面板及光電與自動化及PCB與載板設備等：大銀微的產品應用範圍涵蓋半導體設備(佔比
逾40%)、面板及光電與自動化（佔比約30~35%）、PCB與載板設備（佔比約20%）、精密量測及自動化、生技醫療等
產業，重量級客戶有應材（AMAT）、艾斯摩爾（ASML）、科磊（KLAC）及東京威力科創(8035.T)等全球前四大半導
體設備製造廠。

| 機器人產業簡介 
服務型機器人佔比較⾼，人形機器人正在起步：依據Precedence Research預估，服務型機器人市場2026年為725億美
元，2035年將可增加至2,338億美元，年均複合成⻑率為14%；工業型機器人市場2026年為307億美元，2035年將可增
加至933億美元，年均複合成⻑率為13%；人形機器人市場2026年為22億美元，2035年將可增加至88億美元，年均複
合成⻑率為35%，人形機器人雖然市場成⻑速度較快，但由於正在起步，市場規模及滲透率仍較低。 
機器人市場

資料來源 : Precedence Research、 玉山投顧整理、繪製 
人形機器人的結構與零組件

資料來源 : IEK、 玉山投顧整理、繪製

### Page 5

5 
AI模型讓機器人可持續學習優化：機器人智能可分為三個決策層、執行層及感知層三個主要層級，決策層負責環境理解、
任務規劃與行為決策；執行層由機器人本體與運動控制系統組成，包括伺服馬達、減速機、關節模組與控制器，負責把 
AI 的決策轉換成具體動作；感知層負責蒐集環境資訊，常見設備有RGB或RGB-D攝影機、雷射測距儀、力矩與觸覺感
測器，以及慣性量測單元，透過多模態感測資訊融合，系統才能對環境與物件有更精準的理解；AI模型讓機器人可持續
學習優化，隨著人形機器人與雙臂操作機器人快速發展，機構設計也愈來愈接近人類，如多自由度關節、靈巧⼿等設計，
讓機器人能在既有的人類工作環境中執行更複雜的任務。 
機器人智能系統主要層級包括決策層、執行層與感知層

資料來源 : 台達電 
控制器相當於機器人的大腦，AI時代更形重要：控制器的主要功能包括運動控制(計算多軸反向運動學，實現精準定位與
軌跡追蹤)、信號傳遞(將邏輯決策轉為電氣信號傳給驅動器與馬達)、安全防護(緊急停止與安全監控保護人員與設備)與
系統整合(連接視覺、力覺感測器以及工廠聯網通訊) ，AI時代將更形重要。 
AI時代控制器更形重要

資料來源 : 新代法說會簡報 
人機協作為目前工廠新典型，未來將走向智慧化：人機協作是指人類與人工智慧、機器人或自動化系統共同工作、互補
優勢以達成任務的模式，它透過機器處理大量數據與重複性工作，結合人類的創造力、同理心與終端決策，實現效率與
價值的最大化；核心優勢與分工在人機協作的模式下，人類與機器各司其職，發揮最強效能，機器擅⻑資料快速運算、
例行程序自動化、預測分析與24小時監控，而人類擅⻑戰略⽅向決策、複雜情境判斷、創意發想以及人際同理心溝通；
人機協作的共同效益為大幅降低人力閒置、提升組織生產力，並協助員工從低技術工作轉型為高附加價值工作，人機協
作為目前工廠新典型，但未來將走向智慧化，智慧化機器人結合了AI、先進感測器與精密控制系統，能自主感知周遭環
境、進行即時資料分析、做出決策，並執行原本需要人類智慧與物理動作才能完成的複雜任務，將AI實體化，其核心技

### Page 6

6 
術與特點在於將大腦演算法與實體機械結合，讓機器人具備空間理解與動態適應能力，並具有高精度感測與視覺，可透
過3D深度相機、光達與扭力感測器，做到精準避障與靈敏控制，由於具有模組化關節與驅動，透過高度整合馬達、驅動
器與通訊介面，提升扭力密度與即時反應速度；智慧化機器人主要應用領域智慧製造包括彈性產線、自動化組裝、設備
巡檢與物料搬運；醫療與照護⽅面如⽀援⼿術輔助、醫療物流與高齡化社會的照護陪伴；也能應用於特殊與極端環境，
如進入災害現場、地下空間或危險區域進行偵察與救援。 
協作型機器人市場2034年預估達713億美元

資料來源 : Precedence Research、 玉山投顧整理、繪製 
協作型機器人市場至2034年的年均複合成⻑率為35%：協作型機器人（Cobot）是⼀種專為與人類在共同工作空間內安
全協作而設計的機器人，與傳統工業機器人不同，其設計核心不是「最大化速度與力量」，而是「讓機器能夠安全地在
人的旁邊工作」，協作型機器人的概念最早在 1990 年代由學術界提出，2008年丹麥Universal Robots推出第⼀款商
業化Cobot協作型機器人後，這個品類才真正進入工業應用視野。此後十餘年間，隨著感測技術、控制演算法與製造成
本的成熟，協作型機器人快速普及，從歐美大廠擴展到亞洲製造業的中小型工廠。依據Precedence Research預估，協
作型機器人市場2025年為56億美元，2034年將可增加至713億美元，年均複合成⻑率為35%。 
機器人走向通用化、智能化、自主化

資料來源 : FAUNC、KUKA、ANFAM、世紀貿易，玉山投顧整理、繪製 
傳動元件分類與比較

현대차 (005380)

EARNINGS PREVIEW Company Analysis | 자동차/로봇 | 2026.10.16  
4분기 반등 기대

3Q26 Preview 
현대차는 3Q26E 매출액 42.4조원(-9.4% YoY, -13.9% QoQ), 영업이익 2.5조원(-
2.8% YoY, -13.5% QoQ, OPM 5.8%)을 기록, 컨센서스를 하회할 전망이다. 각 부문
별 영업이익은 자동차 1.7조원(-2.0% YoY, -14.9% QoQ, OPM 4.8%), 금융 5,501억
원(-4.5% YoY, -27.5% QoQ, OPM 6.9%)으로 예상한다. 
생산 정상화 및 신차 확대를 통한 수익성 개선 기대 
현대차는 글로벌 도매 판매 91.8만대(-11.5% 이하 YoY), 중국 제외 판매량은 90.9
만대(-9.6%)를 기록했다. 7~8월에 발생한 파업 영향으로 약 5.5만대 규모의 생산 차
질이 발생했다. 파업 영향으로 HEV 판매 증가율이 1%에 그치면서, HEV 판매 확대에 
따른 믹스 개선 효과도 시장 기대에 미치지 못했다.  
4분기부터는 3분기 파업 영향으로 지연된 생산량 회복 및 신차 확대가 본격화되면서, 
전분기 대비 매출 및 수익성 개선이 기대된다. 동사는 보급형 전기차 아이오닉3의 튀
르키예 생산을 9월부터 시작했으며, 이를 통해 유럽 시장 판매량은 점차 회복될 전망
이다. 또한, 1) HMGMA를 통한 HEV 미국 현지 생산 확대, 2) 제네시스 HEV 모델 순
차적 출시를 통해 내년부터 북미를 중심으로 믹스 개선이 본격화될 전망이다. 
투자의견은 Buy를 유지하고 목표주가는 520,000원으로 하향 
2027년 영업이익은 11.2조원(+10.2%)를 기록할 것으로 예상된다. 원화 약세 지속을 
반영해 이익 추정치를 하향하며, 이에 따라 목표주가를 하향한다. 다만, 현재 주가는 
12M Fwd P/E 기준 6.1배로 Legacy OEM 평균(7.7배)보다 낮은 수준이다. 1) RMAC 
성과 구체화, 2) RA(로봇 생산법인) 착공 등 Physical AI 사업이 구체화될 경우 밸류에
이션 할증이 가능할 전망이다. 
 Analyst 이병근 
bglee@ls-sec.co.kr 
Buy (유지)

Company Report  2026.11.12

상선, 방산, 해양, 엔진 모든 사업 영위하는 전천후 기업 
경쟁사 대비 1) 가장 넓은 사업 영역을 보유, 2) 시가총액 또한 높음. 이러한 특성은 동사의 
사업 포트폴리오 균형 관점에서 장점이나, 반대로 투자 매력의 집중을 일정 부분 희석. 경쟁
사 대비 주가 상승 트리거가 가장 폭넓게 존재하나, 상승 탄력은 그만큼 제한. 
상선: FY27F 매출인식은 FY23-25 수주 물량, 가스선 매출비중 하락 
FY27F 상선부문 매출은 FY23-25 수주 물량이 인식되며 (일부 FY26F 수주), FY25 수주
한 물량이 연간 40% 수준의 가장 높은 매출기여도를 보일 것. 2H27F부터 올해 수주한 일
부 선박의 매출인식 또한 발생하며, 연중 가스선 매출 비중이 하락하고 컨테이너선 매출 비
중이 상승. 
방산: 좋게 보자면 불안하고, 나쁘게 보자면 아쉬운 
MASGA를 국내 야드에서 진행한다는 대전제로 경쟁사와 같은 적정가치를 부여해 왔으나, 
미국 현지 건조와 같은 여러 가능성을 고려하면 다음과 같은 불확실성 존재: 1) 중간지주사
와의 미국내 JV 설립 관련 투자 지분 미정, 2) 신규 조선소 건립시 미국내 수주와 매출까지 
걸리는 시간. 
투자의견 BUY 유지, 다 깎아서 봐도 충분한 상승여력 
상선부문 26.5조, 방산 8조, 선박엔진 9조, 발전엔진 (FY30F 기준) 13.5조 수준의 SOTP 
밸류에이션 기반하여 목표주가 643,000원 제시. 발전엔진 증설로 인한 미래가치를 반영하
나, 상선부문 Target P/O multiple 하향 (0.7x, 업종 공통)과 MASGA 적정가치 하향으로 
목표주가 자체는 하락. 보수적 멀티플 제시함에도 주가 상승 여력은 충분히 확보된 상황. 업
종 전체적 긍정요인 외의 동사 주가 상승 트리거는 1) 미국 현지 조선소 투자주체 발탁, 2) 
DC향 발전엔진 추가 수주, 3) MASGA 국내 건조 물량 수주.

AI IS BOOSTING BUSINESS CREATIONS BOTH IN THE US AND EUROPE
Stéphane Colliac
The significant increase in business creations since mid-2025 in the United States, France and Germany – and, to a lesser extent, in the United
Kingdom – is continuing. Across the board, this trend is being driven by the information and communication sector and mostly by AI. It is
spreading to other business services, while developments in consumer services, industry and construction are more mixed. However, in both

the United States and France, the trend is broadening, suggesting that AI is facilitating business creation across all sectors.

### Page 2

2
The bank for
a changing
world
Chart of the Week / 4 November 2026 economic-research.bnpparibas.com
A noticeable boom in the United States, France, and Germany, but not quite as pronounced in the United Kingdom
The four economies for which we have comparable data (the United States, France, Germany and the United Kingdom) have all
seen, to varying degrees, an increase in the number of new business creations. While the timing varies across these countries,

most of them are enjoying strong momentum. In the United States, there has been a noticeable acceleration since the second
quarter of 2025, which has continued to strengthen. Germany has followed a similar trajectory, moving from slight declines at
the start of 2024 to sharp increases. France is experiencing the same trend but with some specific features, as momentum has
picked up significantly after months of slowdown (due to the uncertainty following the dissolution of the National Assembly) and is

accelerating across most sectors in 2026. In the United Kingdom, the trend is less pronounced and the increase may not last.
Business services, and AI in particular, are driving this momentum
In most of these four countries, business creations are rising significantly in the information and communication sector, which is
directly linked to AI. In the United States, Germany and France, there has been a pronounced trend which is gaining momentum.
In the United Kingdom, the AI sector is also seeing an increase, but it is significantly less pronounced. However, this momentum is

not limited to this sector. It is spreading to many services sectors, including finance, real estate and business services. Growth has
been sustained since mid-2025 in the United States and Germany, and since the third quarter of 2025 in France.
The picture for consumer services is more mixed. In the United States, trade, accommodation and catering have experienced major
increases in creations since mid-2025. In France, these sectors are in positive territory, particularly trade, where the increase in

business creations has recently accelerated. In Germany, these same sectors fell into negative territory in 2026 following a rather
mixed year in 2025.
The situation is even more mixed for manufacturing and construction. In the United States, these sectors are also part of the
overall positive momentum in the third quarter of 2026. This shows that AI does not only benefit sectors closely linked to it
(notably the digital technology sector) but that it makes it easier to start a business across the entire economy (by simplifying

administrative procedures in particular). In France, the upturn in business creations in the construction sector suggests that this
same trend is at play, even as activity in the sector continues to contract.
Therefore, the trend is more widespread in the United States and is becoming increasingly prevalent in France. It is driven by
specific sectors of the services industry in Germany, while it is significantly more subdued in the United Kingdom.

2 of 8 November 16, 2026 – Capital Market Outlook RETURN TO FIRST PAGE
MACRO STRATEGY
Inflation Sticky but Overheating Less Clear
Chief Investment Office, Macro Strategy Team
The disconnect between very strong recent growth with stubborn inflation above the Fed’s 2%
target on one hand and more moderate business surveys and labor-market indicators on the
other, has complicated the optimal Fed policy path and contributed to large swings in interest

rates. Indeed, the economy looks strong in several respects. Real GDP grew at a 3.4% annualized
pace in Q3, led by 3.8% real consumer spending growth, according to Bureau of Economic
Analysis (BEA), surging AI-related business investment, and a 16% annualized increase in real
federal government spending. As policy uncertainty eased from its tariff-related spike and growth
remained resilient, labor data have also improved. Job openings and quits rates have stabilized.

The October payrolls reports exceeded expectations. The unemployment rate, according to
Bureau of Labor Statistics (BLS), slipped to 4.1% from 4.5% in November 2025.
Stronger hiring alongside higher-than-expected October consumer price index (CPI) inflation revived
expectations for further Fed tightening, however, causing renewed sharp interest rate increases.
Yet, as discussed below, the recent strong growth reacceleration followed a period of softer, tariff-

shock-related economic conditions, and some of its driving forces are likely to moderate. Moreover,
inflation still appears disproportionately related to energy, tariffs, freight costs and increasing
AI-related electronics inflation. Tariffs have already started to fade, though, while diesel prices have
potential to decline once geopolitical conflicts resolve. In this context, there is a risk that higher
interest rates would not only overly restrain demand but would also delay investment in housing,

power generation and other capacity expansion needed to keep inflation in check.
Here are a few details underpinning this view:
• The strong Q3 real GDP growth acceleration followed just a 1.6% annualized pace over the preceding
three quarters, with consumer spending also up at a moderate 2.1% average rate. At about 2.1%
and 2.5% year-over-year (YoY) growth, respectively, neither GDP nor consumption growth appear
exceptional, according to BEA. The Chicago Fed National Activity Index, which aggregates information

from 85 economic indicators seems to concur—following three years of subtrend readings, it has
oscillated between slightly above- and below-trend growth territory for most of the year.
• Business surveys also suggest ongoing expansion but not booming growth. The National Federation
of Independent Business Small Business Optimism Index remains below its July level even as job
openings, hiring intentions, and plans to raise worker pay spiked in October. The October Institute

for Supply Management (ISM) manufacturing survey has remained a bright spot, with production and
employment numbers particularly robust. While the overall index is associated with roughly 2.7% real
GDP growth, according to the ISM, its services counterpart has lagged, with below-average readings
across new orders, production, and employment in October. As a result, the weighted average of the
two surveys remains way below levels seen in prior expansions (Exhibit 1), in line with the moderate

growth dynamics pervading the Fed’s October Beige Book, for example.
• According to BLS, hiring was unquestionably strong in October. Payrolls gained 165,000. Hiring in
previous months was revised up. The three-month average jobs growth shifted to 137,000 from just
28,000 in July. Unemployment dropped to 4.1%. Still, part of the improvement represents catch-up
from the hiring lull observed after the tariff shock and related spike in policy uncertainty (Exhibit 2).

Labor turnover remains low, with the quits rate unsurprisingly depressed given the sharp uptrend
in the average duration of unemployment, which increased in October to a five-year high (also a
historically high level).
• Labor supply has also surprised to the upside as employment strengthened, precluding excessive wage
growth outside specific areas of technically skilled labor scarcity. After cratering in the year following
the tariff shock, the labor force participation rate (LFPR) has meaningfully rebounded since July,

helping bring nearly 1.6 million people back looking for work, according to BLS. Indeed, the supply of
labor is partly endogenous to economic conditions—it rises when jobs availability and hiring improve,
and vice versa. Although the prime-age female LFPR is near recent records, a rebound in participation
rates for males age 20 to 54 toward their 1995 to 2000 highs would allow meaningful additional
employment growth before wage-related inflation pressures become an issue.

In itself, renewed hiring following the tariff-related hiatus therefore need not be inflationary. Higher
employment alongside rising labor force participation and solid productivity growth should help
contain unit labor costs. It would also help expand the supply side to better match resilient demand,
helping keep inflation in check and prolonging the expansion.
Investment Implications
We remain overweight Equities,
favor U.S. stocks and see potential

opportunities in broader market
participation and cyclical sectors.
Treasury yields appear attractive for
income generation and diversification
reasons.

### Page 3

3 of 8 November 16, 2026 – Capital Market Outlook RETURN TO FIRST PAGE
• Several supports to recent growth appear unsustainable. Base effects and midterm elections
results suggest a likely lessened growth impetus from AI investment. Faster inventory
accumulation, which significantly boosted Q3 real GDP, cannot continue indefinitely. Also, real
consumer spending barely inched higher in September following an August spike, according to
BEA. Indeed, consumer spending has increasingly outpaced income growth this year, sharply

eroding saving out of income from 6.2% in January to 3.8% in September, limiting the scope
for spending to keep outpacing income. Finally, while Q3 earnings have continued to positively
surprise across most sectors, margins may have limited scope of further expansion from
historically elevated levels. Together, based on our analysis, these factors point toward slower
but still healthy economic growth around 2.3% ahead, according to BofA Global Research.

• According to the BLS, “core” CPI inflation remained faster than desired in October, with
evidence of some broadening. The retreat in Brent oil from roughly $135 to $95 per barrel in
recent weeks has been encouraging, though elevated diesel prices and freight costs are still
spilling into other prices. Also, shelter seems to have run its disinflation course, accounting
for about a third of the October “core” CPI increase as excess rental inventory got absorbed.

Housing-services inflation is now close to its prepandemic average, providing a fading
disinflation tailwind.
All in all, growth is likely to moderate ahead, the tariff effect on inflation is dissipating,
and lower diesel prices would materially relieve near-term inflation pressure. Yet a set
of more structural supply constraints and potential inflation pressures remains to be
addressed. Rising electricity costs, infrastructure bottlenecks, skilled-labor shortages,

an aging population, housing costs and supply limitations, a wealthy segment of the
population relatively insensitive to inflation, disruptive trade and industrial policies, and
the resource demands associated with the AI-investment boom suggest a multitude of
potential inflation pressures if accommodated by persistent large fiscal deficits, excess
credit growth, a wage-cost spiral or unmitigated wealth effects. This strengthens the
case for measures to boost productive capacity and policies to better align nominal

spending with potential supply, if inflation is to sustainably return to target through
increased supply rather than higher reliance on Fed-induced demand suppression.
Restrictive interest rates may in fact delay the very supply response needed to alleviate
some of these inflation pressures.

MARKET VIEW
From Earnings Strength to Earnings Durability
Kirsten Cabacungan, Vice President and Senior Investment Strategist
U.S. Equities have faced a series of crosscurrents in recent months. At the index level, stocks
have seemingly gone nowhere. The S&P 500 has yet to break out of the sideways trading
range that began in early June, rising less than 1% over that period. But that relatively flat

performance masks considerable movement beneath the surface. As enthusiasm around the
AI trade dominated through the summer, market leadership narrowed and breadth steadily
deteriorated. By the end of September, nearly 60% of S&P 500 constituents were more than
15% below their 52-week closing highs, suggesting a sizable correction was already unfolding
beneath the headline index. October and November brought another shift in leadership as
intensifying headwinds around the AI trade pressured mega-cap Technology stocks, while

previously lagging areas began to recover. The Philadelphia Semiconductor Index fell as much
as 38% from its mid-June peak through early November and remains roughly 31% below that
high despite a recent partial recovery.
Against that volatile market backdrop, corporate fundamentals have told a much steadier
story. With roughly 91% of S&P 500 companies having reported as of November 13, Q3
earnings-per-share (EPS) growth is tracking around 32% YoY according to FactSet, which,

if sustained, would mark the third consecutive quarter of earnings growth above 25% and
the eighth consecutive quarter of double-digit growth. The magnitude of earnings growth in
recent quarters is unusual this far into an economic expansion and more closely resembles
the earnings rebounds typically seen following recessions. In fact, outside of the recoveries
following the 2000 Dot-com bubble peak, 2008/2009 Global Financial Crisis and 2020
pandemic, Q2 marked the fastest pace of earnings growth across the last three decades

(Exhibit 2). The underlying results have also remained strong, with revenue growth firmly in
double-digit territory and the Q3 net profit margin tracking its second-highest level since
FactSet began measuring the metric in 2009.
Exhibit 2: Earnings Growth Resembles a Post-Recession Rebound.

Yet equity markets have shown surprisingly little response to that earnings strength. According to

BofA Global Research, companies beating expectations on both earnings and sales outperformed
the S&P 500 by just 90 basis points the following day, compared with a historical average of 1.4
percentage points. Companies missing on both have traded roughly in line with the market, versus
an average historical underperformance of 2.5 percentage points. In turn, the resulting performance
gap between beats and misses is the narrowest since Q4 2000 and Q4 2001, following the March

2000 peak in the Dot-com bubble.1 The disconnect seems to be less about the strength of current
results than the increasingly high bar investors have set for what comes next.
Some moderation from here is almost inevitable. An increasingly difficult YoY comparison
base sets a high hurdle after several quarters of exceptional growth. BofA Global Research
expects S&P 500 EPS growth to slow from a projected 33% in 2026 to 12% in 2027, while
1 BofA Global Research, “Earnings Tracker: Week 4: 2021 beat rate, 2001 price action,” November 6, 2026.

Investment Implications
We continue to favor staying
invested while using periods of
market weakness to rebalance and
broaden portfolio exposure. As
earnings growth moderates and
market leadership becomes more
differentiated, investors should
remain highly diversified and
emphasize higher-quality exposure.

### Page 5

5 of 8 November 16, 2026 – Capital Market Outlook RETURN TO FIRST PAGE
bottom-up consensus remains somewhat more optimistic at 16%. That would represent a
meaningful deceleration, but it would not necessarily signal deterioration as double-digit
earnings growth would still leave the fundamental backdrop on solid footing.
The bigger question for investors though is what could turn that expected earnings growth
normalization into a sharper slowdown. Midterm election results have introduced a new source

of uncertainty about the AI investment cycle, as voters and newly elected Democratic governors
in several states at the center of the data center boom have supported greater restrictions on
development. That risk matters disproportionately for the earnings outlook, with Technology
and Energy accounting for roughly 75% of Q3 S&P 500 earnings growth (Exhibit 3A). BofA
Global Research estimates that a hypothetical 20% reduction in 2027 AI capital spending

could translate into 35% to 40% downside to AI semiconductor EPS and lower S&P 500 EPS
by roughly 7% to 8%, before accounting for secondary effects. So far, however, a slowdown
has yet to emerge in company guidance, with hyperscalers continuing to raise capital spending
plans. While recent market repricing has reflected some of that uncertainty, the durability of AI
investment and earnings remains an important factor in the broader earnings outlook.

For now, the conditions that typically accompany a broader earnings downturn remain largely
absent. Economic activity continues to expand at an above-trend pace, with the Atlanta Fed
GDPNow model tracking 3.5% annualized real gross domestic product growth for Q4. Consumer
spending also remains firm heading into the holiday season. Bank of America aggregated credit
and debit card spending per household rose 5.8% YoY in October, or 4.6% excluding gasoline.2

Although both measures moderated from September, they remain close to the strong growth
rates recorded since the spring. That demand resilience continues to show through corporate
earnings results. According to FactSet, 84% of S&P 500 companies have reported actual EPS
above mean estimates, well above the five-year average of 78%. And that strength extends well
beyond the largest contributors to index earnings growth, with nine of the 11 S&P 500 sectors

seeing 80% or more of reporting companies exceed consensus EPS estimates (Exhibit 3B) and
all sectors tracking positive earnings growth for 2026. While the earnings cycle may be moving
beyond its strongest phase, the broader backdrop does not yet point to a fundamental downturn.
For investors, a moderating earnings backdrop should place greater emphasis on where
earnings growth is coming from and how durable that growth ultimately proves. Greater

market dispersion should favor companies with healthy operating cash flows, strong balance
sheets and the ability to translate investment into sustainable returns. That applies particularly
to the AI buildout. A slower or more disciplined pace of capital spending would not necessarily
signal an end to the investment cycle but could help separate the companies and projects that
can ultimately deliver on their investment from those where expectations have moved too

far, too fast. That type of differentiation should become increasingly important as the market
moves into a more measured phase of the cycle.

THOUGHT OF THE WEEK
Mercantilist China Powers On: The Relentless Rise of Its Trade Surplus
Ariana Chiu, Assistant Vice President and Investment Strategist
And the boom continues. If investors have learned anything this year, it is that China’s not
ready to adjust its mercantilist growth model. Even in the face of higher U.S. tariffs and a
corresponding decline in exports to the U.S., China recorded a staggering $1T merchandise

trade surplus in just the first 10 months of this year. By the end of 2026, the country’s
trade surplus is on track to surpass 2025’s record and reach something closer to $1.3T3—or
triple the level at the start of the decade ($421 billion4) (Exhibit 4A).
Who is on the receiving end of all these exports? Hint: not the U.S., whose tariffs
taught the rest of the world that it can no longer count on America to absorb a significant
share of China’s exports. Per Exhibit 4B, as recently as 2019, the U.S. accounted for 20% of

exports from China. Today, that share is 10%, down sharply from 15% pre-Liberation Day.
That’s been a bitter pill to swallow for trading partners like the EU and Association of
Southeast Asian Nations (ASEAN) countries, whose year-to-date deficits with China have
ballooned by 29% and 34% YoY, respectively. Germany, whose local manufacturing industry
continues to struggle under the weight of competition from the mainland, has seen its
deficit with China double in just the last year. It’s no surprise, then, that the chorus of

complaints against China’s mercantilist approach continues to swell beyond Washington.
From quotas on electric vehicle imports in the EU to anti-dumping investigations in India to
product-specific tariffs in Mexico, Brazil, and South Africa, the world is pushing back against
China and its well-subsidized industries.
Looking ahead to 2027, a full-blown tit-for-tat global trade war is far from our base
case. However, we wouldn’t be surprised to see more targeted tariffs, investigations, and

industrial subsidies from developed and emerging markets alike, especially in strategic
sectors like autos/parts, chemicals, steel and basic metals, and other manufactured goods.
In the U.S., despite the Democratic sweep at the midterm elections, tariffs are likely here to
stay via legal measures like Section 232 and Section 301.
For China, more pushback from the rest of the world could force the economy to finally
come to terms with persistently depressed domestic demand. Meanwhile, investment

in domestic manufacturing should continue in the U.S. and Europe. We favor the former
over the latter in portfolios because the diversified nature of the U.S. economy, existing
protectionist measures, and tilt toward services and innovation leave growth relatively less
exposed to competition in manufacturing.
Exhibit 4: Taking Stock of China’s #1 Growth Engine.

### Page 1

A New Keynesian ‘plucking’ model of unemployment and inflation
PRELIMINARY AND INCOMPLETE
Anthony Brassil* and Callum Ryan*
Research Discussion Paper
20XX-YY
December 2025
*Economic Research Department
Reserve Bank of Australia
The views expressed in this paper are those of the authors and should not be attributed to the
Reserve Bank of Australia. Any errors are the sole responsibility of the authors.
Author: ryanca at domain rba.gov.au
External Communications: rbainfo@rba.gov.au

https://doi.org/10.47688/rdp20XX-YY

### Page 2

Abstract
We construct a New Keynesian model with search-and-matching frictions in the labour market that
can generate the nonlinear ‘plucking’ dynamics of unemployment in the data: unemployment tends to
spike upwards and then slowly decline. The model features endogenous layoffs, a ‘slippery’ job ladder,
downward nominal wage rigidity, and convex recruiting costs. It generates both the ‘deepness’ and
‘steepness’ asymmetries in unemployment dynamics, and broadly matches the response of labour-

market flows in a downturn. The model endogeneously generates a Phillips curve relationship that
is strongly nonlinear and state-dependent, and a short-run non-accelerating rate of unemployment
(NAIRU) that fluctuates asymmetrically over the business cycle. We also show that cyclical properties
(e.g. shock volatility, nominal rigidities, policy regime) can have a significant effect on the long-run
NAIRU via agents’ precautionary response to future uncertainty.

Keywords:

Figure 1: Unemployment rate in Australia
*Long-run NAIRU estimate from Ballantyne and Cusbert (2025).
1. Introduction
The unemployment rate in the data has strongly nonlinear dynamic (Figure 1). It spikes sharply in
occasional downturns, then declines slowly but persistently in recoveries.1 It is right-skewed in both
levels (i.e. asymmetric ‘deepness’) and in changes (i.e. asymmetric ‘steepness’) (Sichel 1993). Gross
flows between unemployment and employment display similar nonlinearities. Standard search-and-

matching models of the labour market struggle to generate these nonlinearities in unemployment
and labour-market flows (Ferraro 2023).
If this asymmetric pattern stems from frictions within the labour market, what implication do those
frictions have for the relationship between inflation and unemployment? Research using nonlinear
structural models to explore the asymmetric dynamics in the labour market tend to abstract from price
frictions and inflation dynamics (e.g. Dupraz, Nakamura and Steinsson 2025; Ferraro 2018; Petrosky-

Nadeau, Zhang and Kuehn 2018), while in the New Keynesian literature, the usual approach of
linearising equilibrium conditions excludes these nonlinear dynamics by assumption. The 2020s
inflation surge has reignited research into the nonlinearity of the Phillips curve, and the extent to which
this nonlinearity stems from frictions within the labour market (e.g. Benigno and Eggertsson 2023).
But this literature tends not to model the labour market in as much detail.

In this paper, we explore the extent to which a detailed search-and-matching model of the labour
market with pricing frictions can match the nonlinear dynamics in the labour market. We then use
the model to examine the implications of these nonlinearities for the Phillips curve relationship and
the non-accelerating rate of unemployment (NAIRU).
1 Friedman (1964) uses the ‘plucking’ description for the asymmetric way in which output seems to be occasionally ‘plucked’

below trend, before recovering back to trend.

### Page 5

2
We start with a general-equilibrium search-and-matching model similar to Bloesch, Lee and
Weber (2024), in which firms post wages and vacancies and set prices (subject to nominal rigidities
and recruiting costs), and workers search (while unemployed and on the job) and decide whether to
accept and quit jobs. Policy steers aggregate demand to balance inflation and unemployment targets.
We extend this model to include endogenous layoffs, downward nominal wage rigidity, and a ‘slippery’

job ladder (drawing on Dupraz et al (2025)).2 We find that these features - together with convex
recruiting costs - are collectively key to achieving both the asymmetric spikes in unemployment when
shocks occur and the slow speed in the subsequent recovery. The model is sufficiently detailed to
broadly match the dynamics of unemployment and gross flows, but tractable enough that we can
obtain the global nonlinear solution with rational expectations and aggregate uncertainty. This is

important because agents’ precautionary response to future uncertainty is a significant determinant
of outcomes, especially the long-run NAIRU.
We show that the model can generate substantial asymmetry in both ‘deepness’ and ‘steepness’
(comparable to or greater than in the data). The response of unemployment, job-finding, and
separations after a stylised negative demand shock broadly matches the response of these variables in
downturns over the past 50 years. Aggregate demand shocks have an especially asymmetric effect,

because the effect on inflation amplifies the effect of the downward nominal wage rigidity. When
supply shocks occur, the inflation response somewhat eases the downward nominal wage constraint.
The model endogenously generates a Phillips curve relationship that is strongly nonlinear and state-
dependent. Conditional on some initial state of the economy, the Phillips curve is much steeper for
low rates of unemployment. When the initial unemployment rate is higher, the Phillips curve shifts to

the right, and when the initial level of real wages is higher, it shifts upwards.
We can also use the model to simulate a ‘short-run NAIRU’, which is the unemployment rate consistent
with eliminating inflationary pressure from the labour market at some cyclical horizon (e.g. 1-2 years).
This short-run NAIRU varies asymmetrically with the state of the economy. It tends to rise when
unemployment is high, but also when unemployment has fallen rapidly (because real wages increase).

Finally, we can use the ‘stochastic steady state’ of the model to explore the determinants of the long-
run NAIRU, i.e. the unemployment rate consistent with stable inflation in the absence of shocks (but
where agents still expect that shocks may occur in the future). We find that when firms account for
the interaction between aggregate risk and asymmetric wage adjustment costs in their wage-setting
decisions, it generates a trade-off between unemployment variance and steady-state unemployment.

For example, if policy places greater weight on reducing the gap between unemployment and the
long-run NAIRU (instead of the gap between inflation and target), it tends to increase the long-run
NAIRU. This is because by mitigating fluctuations in labour demand, policy reduces the risk of hitting
downward nominal wage rigidities. Firms are then more willing to set higher real wages in steady
state, which reduces the optimal amount of employment in steady state.

The remainder of this introduction discusses the literature. Section 2 outlines the model. Section 3
evaluates the extent to which it can match the nonlinear dynamics in labour-market data. Section 4
considers the implications for the Phillips curve and NAIRU.
2 By ‘slippery’ job ladder, we mean that workers recently hired out of unemployed are much more likely to be laid off.

### Page 6

3
Literature review. The cyclical asymmetries in unemployment are established empirically by e.g.
Sichel (1993), McKay and Reis (2008), and Dupraz et al (2025). Ferraro (2023) analyses the ability of
the baseline Diamond-Mortensen-Pissarides (DMP) model to produce these asymmetries, and finds
that it cannot generate the ‘steepness’ asymmetry (i.e. right-skewness in unemployment changes).
Petrosky-Nadeau et al (2018) finds that the DMP model can generate the ‘deepness’ asymmetry given

large enough shocks.
Dupraz et al (2025) and Ferraro (2018) propose search-and-matching models that can generate
sharp spikes and slow declines in unemployment. We take several features from Dupraz et al (2025),
including downward nominal wage rigidity and a ‘slippery’ job ladder. Pizzinelli, Theodoridis and
Zanetti (2020) explore the state-dependence of unemployment fluctuations in a model with
endogenous layoffs and on-the-job search.3 Adjemian, Karamé and Langot (2021) estimate a

nonlinear model with endogenous separations, downward wage rigidity, worker heterogeneity and
segmented markets, and show that it can generate similar asymmetries in unemployment, hiring,
and separations to the data.
Our definition of a ‘short-run NAIRU’ is similar to the concept discussed by Hall and Kudlyak (n.d.), who
argue that the natural rate of unemployment may vary cyclically and track the actual unemployment
rate during recoveries. Hall and Kudlyak (2022) discusses the ability of DMP-style models to generate

the slow persistent recovery in unemployment that occurs after recessions. Fujita and Ramey (2007)
show that adding vacancy adjustment costs to a DMP model can produce a more sluggish response
for vacancies and hiring after a downturn, slowing down the recovery in unemployment. Mercan,
Schoefer and Sedláček (2024) suggest a congestion mechanism, whereby new hires and incumbent
workers are imperfect substitutes in production, which also propagates unemployment after a shock.

This literature above generally abstracts from price rigidities and inflation dynamics. There is a
substantial literature incorporating labour-market frictions into New Keynesian models (see e.g.
Blanchard and Galí 2010). Recent examples include Moscarini and Postel-Vinay (2023), Faccini and
Melosi (2025), and Bloesch et al (2024). These papers focus on the role of on-the-job search and wage
competition between firms in inflation dynamics. We adopt a similar model for wage setting, drawing

on the tractable formulation in Bloesch et al (2024). But these papers use linear approximations.
The literature on downward nominal wage rigidity explores the extent to which it generates a
nonlinear Phillips curve and asymmetric cyclical dynamics (see e.g. Kim and Ruge-Murcia 2009;
Abbritti and Fahr 2013; Daly and Hobijn 2014; Benigno and Antonio Ricci 2011; Schmitt-Grohé
and Uribe 2022; Benigno and Eggertsson 2023; Benigno and Eggertsson 2024). Lepetit (2020)

also constructs a New Keynesian model with asymmetric unemployment dynamics, although the
asymmetry stems from the convex relationship between the job-finding rate and unemployment.
This literature does not model the labour market in as much detail (e.g. endogenous layoffs, on-the-
job search, job ladder) and does not seek to match the ‘plucking’ property in unemployment to the
same extent.
3 Fujita and Ramey (2012) also analyse versions of the DMP model with endogenous separations and on-the-job search,

but their focus is not on asymmetries in unemployment rate dynamics

### Page 7

4
2. Model
The model consists of firms, workers, and a policy rule. We start by outlining the firms’ problem
and optimal choices. Firms’ optimal decision-making drives most of the model’s mechanics. We then
discuss workers and the policy regime, which are modelled more simply. 5 contains a full list of
equilibrium conditions, as well as details of the calibration and solution method.
2.1 Firms
Firms make decisions around hiring, separations, and price- and wage-setting.

Hiring: Firms choose how many vacancies to post, V i t . These vacancies generate Mi t = qtV i t matches,
where qt is determined by a matching function and depends on aggregate matching efficiency and
labour market tightness. A proportion 1 − ϕ E t of these matches are unemployed searchers (where ϕ E t
is the share of unemployed searchers in total searchers, weighted by search intensity).
Firms must screen matches. Then the individual productivities of employed matches are revealed.

Firms make offers to those with productivities above some threshold xi t , which is 1−F i t of them (where
F i t = F(xi t ) and F is the CDF of the individual productivity distribution). For unemployed matches,
firms have no information about their individual productivity and make offers to all of them. This
feature proxies for the idea that firms may be more uncertain about the capabilities of unemployed
applicants.
Matches with offers choose whether to accept them; Ace t (W i t ) and employed matches and Acu t (W i t )

of unemployed matches do so, where W i t is the real wage paid by the firm. These job-acceptance
probabilities are characterised in the description of workers below.
The number of new hires is given by
Hi t = ϕ E t Mi t (1 − F i t )Ace t (W i t ) + (1 − ϕ E t )Mi t Acu t (W i t ).
Define Hi,u t = (1 − ϕ E t )Mi t Acu t (W i t ) as the number of hires from unemployment.
Recruiting costs include a per-vacancy cost and a per-match ‘screening’ cost:

Separations: There are two types of separation: quits and layoffs. Each period, Qt (W i t ) of firms’
existing employees quit (either to unemployment or to another job). Just as firms’ hiring rate is

increasing in the real wage they offer (via the job-acceptance probabilities Ace t (W i t ) and Acu t (W i t )),
their quits rate is decreasing in the real wage by the firm. This function is characterised in the section
below on workers.
Workers’ probability of being laid off depends on whether they are insecurely or securely attached.
Insecurely attached employees are all those who have just been hired out of unemployed in
the previous period. Before aggregate shocks are revealed, some portion of new hires from

### Page 8

5
unemployment, δ u, (i.e. who were hired the previous period), draw a new productivity from
distribution G. Those whose productivities are less than some threshold xi,m t−1 are laid off, which
is Gi t−1 of them (where Gi t = G(xi,m t−1) and G is the CDF of the distribution).4 All insecurely attached
employees who are not laid off then because securely attached (together with all those who have
been employed for more than one period).
After aggregate shocks are revealed and quitting decisions made, δ of securely attached employees

draw a new individual productivity. Firms then decide to lay off those whose productivities are less
than some threshold xi t , which is F i t of them (where F i t = F(xi t ) and F is the CDF of the individual
productivity distribution). The layoff probability for securely attached employees will be lower than
for insecurely attached employees both because δ < δ u and the distribution F has less mass at low
values than G.

2.2 Workers
All workers who are unemployed when shocks are revealed search for jobs. A share λ EE of employed

Those who enter unemployment after shocks are revealed, either because they are laid off or because

they quit to unemployment, do not search in that period.6
A share λ EU of employed workers consider quitting to unemployment.
6 This means that even if hiring costs went to zero, unemployment would remain above zero. If the newly unemployed

Workers hired last period who are laid off before shocks are revealed can search. This is to avoid Hu t−1 or Nt−1 being a

state variable.

Monetary policy: Policy tries to implement a flexible inflation target, but is subject to various shocks
(e.g. it cannot fully offset all demand shocks immediately). Therefore:

where Mt is an exogenous nominal demand shock.
2.4 Equilibrium, solution and calibration
We assume that firms have rational expectations, which implies that they understand the interaction
between nonlinearities in the economy and aggregate risk when forming their expectations. This
means, for example, that in the presence of downward nominal wage rigidity, firms will set lower
wages even in steady state, given the risk that it will be very costly for them to reduce wages if

negative shocks occur in the future.
We compute the global solution to the nonlinear model, so that we can explore how the interaction
between nonlinearities and aggregate risk affects expectations and dynamics. The state variables are
employment ˜Nt−1 (after insecurely attached employees are laid off), real wages Wt−1, the average
idiosyncratic productivity of employees ¯xt−1, and the aggregate productivity and demand shocks, At
and Mt .
The model is calibrated so that steady-state values for unemployment and key labour-market flows

are broadly similar to their values Australian data around 2025, as well as to match the standard
deviation of the unemployment rate over 1980 to 2019 and generate a similar positive skewness of
unemployment rate changes.
See Appendix 5 for more detail on the calibration and solution method.
3. How well can we match the dynamics of the labour market in the data?
3.1 Comparing the model to labour-market dynamics in the data
In this section, we compare the dynamics of unemployment and labour-market flows in the model

to Australian data. Figure 2 illustrates the path of transition rates and flows between labour-market
states in Australia since 1980. It shows that the UE rate is negative correlated with unemployment,
even though UE flows (as a proportion of the labour force) are positively correlated. UE flows increase
when the pool of unemployed is larger, but this increase is not enough for the UE rate to increase.

### Page 14

11
Figure 2: Labour-market transition rates and flows in Australia
Note: The UE and EU flows in the data are divided by the labour force size to make them comparable to
the model flows. The shaded regions represent contractions, which are identified using the algorithm in
Dupraz et al (2025).
Figure 3: Comparing recessions in the model and the data
Note: The UE and EU flows in the data are divided by the labour force size to make them comparable to the model

flows.
The figure also shows that the EU rate and EU flows are postively correlated with unemployment,
and the EE rate (i.e. job-to-job transition rate) is negatively correlated (at a cyclical frequency).
Figure 3 compares a stylised recession in our model to recessions in the data since 1980.9 The model
downturn is generated with a sequence of six demand shocks (over six months) calibrated to produce
a similar path for unemployment to the downturns in the data. The paths for transition rates and

flows from the model are broadly similar to the data. In particular, the UE rate falls while UE flows
increase, and the magnitude of all responses is comparable to the data.
9 We exclude the 2010-14 and 2022-25 upswings because they involved a gradual increase in unemployment rather than
a spike.

### Page 15

12
Next, we consider whether our model can reproduce the ‘plucking’ or asymmetric ‘sharp spike’ and
‘slow decline’ patterns we see in the unemployment rate data. One measure of this pattern is the
skewness of unemployment rate changes. In the data, this moment is quite positive, reflecting
frequent small decreases and occasional large increases (Table 1) (Sichel (1993) called this a
‘steepness’ asymmetry). Standard search-and-matching models tend to generate negative skewness

in unemployment changes (Ferraro 2023). In contrast, under the current calibration, simulations of
our model generate a substantially positive skewness of unemployment rate changes, although not
quite as large as in the data.
We can also compare other moments of the unemployment rate and moments of the unemployment-
to-employment (UE) and employment-to-unemployment (EU) transition rates in our model to the
data. The model generates somewhat higher variance and negative skewness in the UE rate, whereas

the UE rate is positively skewed in Australian data over this sample period. In US data, the UE rate
has higher variance and is negatively skewed (see Appendix A.3). The model matches the variance
and skewness of the EU rate well, but generates more skewness in changes in the EU rate than in
the data.

Unemployment rate UE rate EU rate
